- Self-reflection 2026-07-23: Hermes learned that probing existing tool wiring before installing LM Studio shims, sourcing partner CLI paths in start.ps1, and respecting repo pre-push Phase 0 doctrine prevents rework and keeps windows/mac/lan comms valid.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.9 evidence=2 id=lesson_retro_20260723 -->

### 2026-06

## Auto-promoted entries will be appended below

### 2026-08

- ~~Handwaving a known error always costs more later than fixing it now. When a check surfaces something that looks minor, tangential, or "not what I'm here to fix," the instinct to note it and move on is usually wrong -- the cost doesn't disappear, it just relocates to whoever hits it next (often the same agent, in a later, more expensive context). Treat every surfaced finding as either fixed now or explicitly, visibly deferred with a stated reason -- never silently absorbed into "I'll get to it."~~  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_0b3f101508e4 superseded_by=lesson_502211a1be56 -->
- ~~Append-only historical records (lessons, audits, vulnerability memory, review ledgers, and any file whose value depends on an intact record of what was actually claimed and when) must never be rewritten in place, even to fix a real error in the original entry. A direct edit destroys the original claim with no queryable trace within the data itself of what it used to say -- exactly the auditability the record exists to provide. The correct fix: append a new entry that supersedes the original (a supersedes/superseded_by back-reference), never mutate the original's fields. This applies to the record itself, not to copies rendered or cached from it -- a machine-generated markdown file regenerated from a corrected append-only source is fine to regenerate in place, and non-historical documents (working docs, prose restating a claim) can be edited directly.~~  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_8ebfd5447e38 superseded_by=lesson_e773f6f957c2 -->
- PR body updates are append-only — ManagePullRequest update_pr and gh pr edit replace the entire body. After every commit on a branch with an open PR, run bash scripts/git/remind-pr-body-append-only.sh before push; use scripts/cursor/append-pr-body.sh for writes. Documented clobber incidents: PT#154, orama#222, PT#298, orama#239, PT#314; treat ~5x undocumented silent clobbers as likely. PR_BODY_GUARD_STRICT=1 blocks publish until PR_BODY_UPDATE_ACK=1.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.9 evidence=1 id=lesson_a8f3c2e91d04 -->
- When resolving real overlap between a new draft and an existing, more comprehensive doctrine, "avoid duplication" does not automatically mean "make the newer, simpler thing subordinate to the older, more complex one." It means determining which document actually serves which situation and sizing each to its own job. A heavyweight protocol built for a rare, hard problem (e.g. concurrent multi-agent edits to the same repo, or reconciling a fork separated from its source by months of drift) should not become the default an agent reads first for the common, simple case (one branch, one agent, no concurrent editing) just because it existed first and is more thorough. Getting this backwards is not caught by re-reading a project's own stated single-source-of-truth value, since that value is genuinely being honored in some sense (no literal duplication) even while the sizing is wrong -- it may require direct correction from a human collaborator who can see the actual audience mismatch.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_718c9430f44b -->
- Several tooling and API gotchas recur across a working session even after being individually caught once, because a single encounter doesn't automatically generalize into a standing rule: the PR-list API's merged field being unreliable (must check the single-PR endpoint); squash-merged branches never showing as git ancestors (verify by content/ID presence, not ancestry); a stale local checkout after an earlier push in the same session diverging silently; and diff-scoped CI lint checking a whole touched file, not just the changed hunk. Each of these needs to be treated as a standing checklist item to consult before the relevant operation, not a one-off lesson learned and then re-derived from scratch the next time it's encountered.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_2d53056e593c -->
- When syncing attribution guards across PT, orama, and AlphaClaw, fix PT on its single open guard PR first, then byte-copy only GUARD_SYNC manifest paths to the one open PR per downstream repo — never open fragmented one-file PRs and never run orama-to-PT sync until PT canonical merges, or stale orama copies overwrite PT improvements.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_2f59523a02e3 -->
- Before running sync-attribution-guard-scripts.sh, run check-guard-sync-divergence.sh --workspace; abort when any sibling guard file differs from orama HEAD and the sibling blob is absent from orama history — that means the sibling is ahead and blind sync would clobber improvements. Promote sibling to orama on the single open PR per repo, then sync downstream.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_200af7108094 -->
- Layer 0 for Cursor agents: never automatically change an existing PR description — use ManagePullRequest post_comment or gh pr comment only; hooks block update_pr with body=, gh pr edit, and append-pr-body.sh  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_696cf70a34bd -->
- ~~When a human explicitly authorizes a PR body edit, set CURSOR_PR_BODY_HUMAN_OVERRIDE_ACK=1 then follow append-only Layers 1-6 (READ backup MERGE full body via append-pr-body.sh); delta-only writes remain forbidden~~  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_4c914783ec46 superseded_by=lesson_d1f3789d4592 -->
- Guard-sync waves use one open PR per repo (PT then orama then AlphaClaw); run check-guard-sync-divergence.sh --workspace before sync-attribution-guard-scripts.sh; never open parallel one-file guard PRs  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_e8ae3f3b9fa6 -->
- Cloud turn-end update PR must mean post_comment not update_pr body — treating description field as a scratch pad erases Summary template sections and CodeRabbit tails  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c9ff6cc2625d -->
- When markdownlint-cli2 v0.17.2 lints explicit file globs in CI, root .markdownlint.json filter overrides do not apply to catalog SKILL.md files outside the skills tree — add a per-directory .markdownlint-cli2.jsonc (e.g. line_length 500 for bin/orama-system/) and delete conflicting .markdownlint.json.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_64e972d330c2 -->
- Guard-sync divergence checks must discover workspace siblings via git -C path rev-parse --show-toplevel (not test -d path/.git), reject surplus CLI arguments with exit 2, fail-closed when check-guard-sync-divergence.sh is missing, and scan the full outgoing pre-push commit range for scripts/git/ touches.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_82c94865243e -->
- PR body Layer 0: Cursor agents comment only (post_comment or gh pr comment). Body writes require an operator TTY grant via grant-pr-body-human-override.sh; only append-pr-body.sh is allowed after grant — never update_pr body=, gh pr edit, gh api body mutations, or agent-forgeable env exports.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_8c5f6349aa87 -->
- PR-body operator grant v2 replay state machine: append-pr-body.sh must reserve nonce before gh pr edit, mark-applied only after remote success, consume last; release reservation on edit failure; reconcile CLI consumes when follow-up block already on remote (crash recovery). Never consume before gh pr edit succeeds.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_cd51f7e6bf6c -->
- operator-grant-v2 HMAC canonical payload is fixed-order UTF-8 bytes grant-v2|repo|pr|nonce|issued_at|action|content_digest with no trailing newline; mint and verify must use identical bytes; golden vector test locks regression.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_720dedfdd2c8 -->
- Same-user Keychain HMAC for PR-body grants is escalation control not cryptographic human identity — agent shells with PTY can read the secret or ack file; MVP raises effort; WebAuthn deferred to security-sentinel v2.1.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_446162929cb3 -->
- Operator-gated PR body edits require operator-grant-v2 HMAC ack from grant-pr-body-human-override.sh matching append bytes -- not CURSOR_PR_BODY_HUMAN_OVERRIDE_ACK env exports or operator-grant-v1 plaintext ack. The HMAC grant authorizes the append-pr-body.sh capability itself; it does not prove human identity or presence -- the underlying grant script's TTY-presence check is not a reliable human-vs-agent signal.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_d1f3789d4592 -->
- Tracked .agent memory and working chronicles must not embed ephemeral scratch path literals — repo_hygiene flags them like workstation home paths and blocks PT CI.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_1a6dc6bccad0 -->
- Human-authorized PR body edits require operator-grant-v2 HMAC ack from grant-pr-body-human-override.sh matching append bytes — not CURSOR_PR_BODY_HUMAN_OVERRIDE_ACK env exports or operator-grant-v1 plaintext ack.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_df758c2d1245 -->
- When a markdownlint (or similar whole-file linter) violation surfaces in a file pulled into scope by an unrelated edit, the correct response depends on the file's nature, not a blanket policy: machine-rendered, append-only historical records (a lessons/decisions log where entries are added mechanically over time, meant to be terse and grow indefinitely) should be excluded from the specific rule that doesn't meaningfully apply (e.g. line-length on a log that append-only tooling writes one dense entry per line), via a scoped config override -- not hand-reformatted, since doing so fights the tool that generates the file. Genuinely hand-authored prose (a narrative doc, a plan, a README) should have violations fixed properly, even if the count is nontrivial, since the rule is actually serving its purpose there. Determine which kind of file it is by reading its actual content and structure (does it read as authored narrative, or as accumulated terse entries with a consistent machine-written shape) before choosing exclude vs. fix, not by file location or extension alone.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_a791e2ee5ad6 -->
- When a code review lists multiple findings, checking each one against the actual current file content before fixing it is not a formality -- in a real remediation pass, roughly half of a review's findings can already be correctly handled in current code (fixed by an earlier commit, or never actually present as described), and treating every finding as needing action wastes effort and risks introducing unnecessary or even incorrect changes to code that was already right. The check itself is cheap (grep or read the exact lines cited); skipping it in favor of blindly applying every suggested diff is the expensive mistake.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_9cc7ae1a5f12 -->
- A security guard that inspects shell commands by splitting on command separators (&&, ;, ||) to check each resulting segment independently must also split on bare newlines -- a shell interprets a newline as a command separator too, and a guard that omits it leaves an exploitable gap: a legitimate-looking command on the first line can cause the guard to short-circuit to ALLOW, leaving a malicious second command hidden on the next line completely uninspected. When testing such a guard's segment-splitting logic, the test must exercise the actual bypass condition (the legitimate first command's branch must be genuinely capable of returning ALLOW, e.g. via a properly-mocked active authorization state) -- a test where the first segment already denies on its own passes regardless of whether the fix is present, proving nothing about the real vulnerability.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_4c65d5956822 -->
- A conditional guard like `if [ -f "$script" ] && command -v "$interpreter" >/dev/null; then run "$script"; else run_fallback; fi` silently downgrades security coverage whenever the interpreter is missing but the primary script exists -- it falls through to a narrower fallback path without any signal that the stronger check was skipped. The fallback branch should be reserved for the case the comment actually describes (the primary script itself being absent, e.g. an old clone that predates it); a present-but-unrunnable primary script should fail loudly and closed, not silently downgrade to a weaker check.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_5fb495abcfbc -->
- When a config fix for a CI tool (linter, scanner, formatter) is verified locally before pushing, verifying against a locally-installed "latest" or default version of that tool is not sufficient -- CI workflows commonly pin a specific action/tool version (e.g. a GitHub Action tagged @v19) that can be substantially older than whatever installs by default locally, and newer tool versions can silently support config schema or CLI flags that the older, actually-pinned version does not. A config change that tests clean locally can still fail in real CI for this reason alone. Before trusting a local verification of a CI-tool config fix, identify the exact tool version the CI workflow actually pins (check the action's own release notes or its own bundled dependency versions, often visible in error message doc-links CI itself produces) and install that exact version locally to test against, not the newest available.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_77ce859d6970 -->
- Secret-scanning tools like gitleaks scan the full commit history range in a branch/PR by default, not just the current tree state -- a test fixture or example value that merely resembles a secret (e.g. a hardcoded hex digest used as an expected HMAC output) can trip a generic entropy detector, and even after a later commit fixes the pattern by removing the literal value, the scanner keeps flagging the earlier, now-superseded commit on every subsequent CI run for that branch, because the flagged content is still present in that commit's diff within history. This is a real, recurring category of false positive distinct from an actual leaked secret, and the correct fix is a scoped allowlist entry for the specific commit SHA(s) in the scanner's config (with `useDefault = true` or equivalent to keep the rest of the default ruleset intact) -- not editing the current file again, since the flagged content is no longer there, and not disabling the scanner or its ruleset broadly.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_084412e4e566 -->
- A short/abbreviated git commit SHA (e.g. from `git log --oneline` output) must never be extended into a full 40-character SHA by guessing, padding, or pattern-completing the remaining hex digits -- even when the extension is meant only for a config file (an allowlist, a pin) rather than executable code, a fabricated SHA silently fails to match anything (a security tool's allowlist entry that doesn't match the real commit provides zero actual exemption, while looking correct at a glance) or, worse, could coincidentally collide with an unrelated real commit. Always resolve a full SHA explicitly with `git rev-parse <short-sha>` and use that exact output, verified by direct comparison, before writing it into any tracked file.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_5f0362a3f95f -->
- When two changes bundled in the same commit are meant to work together (a feature-gating flag and the feature it gates, a deferral/rollout date and the behavior that respects it), verify they are actually consistent with each other by running the specific test that exercises that consistency together -- not just trusting each change's own isolated test coverage. A change that extends or modifies a gate without checking what depends on that gate's current state can silently ship an internal contradiction that only surfaces once something exercises both halves at once.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_61d719978435 -->
- When removing a duplicate call site as a concurrency/correctness fix, check whether that call site actually serves two different purposes depending on which code state it runs in before deleting it everywhere -- the same fallback or retry call can be genuine duplication in one state (e.g. a single-racer scenario where the fallback target is already the racer that just failed) and the intended, necessary behavior in a different state (e.g. a multi-racer scenario where the fallback target was never itself one of the racers). Removing it unconditionally fixes the duplication bug in the first state while introducing a regression in the second; the correct fix is usually conditional on which state actually applies, not a blanket removal.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_64d1deca2fca -->
- Confirming a large batch of test failures is pre-existing and unrelated to a session's own changes (via a before/after comparison) is a necessary check but not the same as understanding why the failures exist -- stopping there leaves a large, alarming-looking failure count in the record with no actual explanation. Where time allows, finishing the diagnosis by installing missing dependencies one at a time and re-running (watching the count drop) can turn "pre-existing, unrelated, unexplained" into "pre-existing, unrelated, and here is exactly why, with zero project-level fix needed" -- a materially more useful record for whoever encounters the same large failure count next and would otherwise have to re-investigate it from scratch.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_8bf6b00d2a44 -->
- For the periscope repo, ALWAYS run Go tests with 'CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -tags fts5 ./...' (per the repo's own Makefile and .github/workflows/ci.yml -- every real invocation uses -tags fts5, several add more tags like pgtest/s3test/evalingest for specific suites). Never run plain 'go test ./...' there -- it silently disables the FTS5 sqlite module (mattn/go-sqlite3 needs the build tag to compile in fts5 support), producing spurious 'no such table: messages_fts' / phase-message-ordering failures that look like real bugs but are just a missing build tag.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_3fe9ebcaf9e0 -->
- ~~An agent renaming a directory/binary must never also infer a broader identity change (module path, package namespace, org identity) in the same commit unless explicitly authorized — each identity-bearing change needs its own explicit human sign-off, even when bundled with an authorized structural rename~~  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_0c339d39a80c superseded_by=lesson_d7f233fdd47e -->
- Never judge a config value 'stale' or 'correct' relative to an upstream/grandmother repo from memory or from your own fork's history alone — fetch the live upstream file (raw.githubusercontent.com or equivalent) directly before asserting what it currently contains  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_aedbe2d7db06 -->
- Undetected agentic scope-creep can be fully recovered days later without a history rewrite: trace the exact origin commit via git log --follow/blame, verify the true baseline against live upstream sources, then land a mechanical forward revert on a fresh branch with full compile and test reverification -- never force-push or rebase through already-shared history to 'undo' it  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_7620f39c87bf -->
- Hermes dispatch has three lanes: L-H1 native delegate_task children, L-PT PT spawn_hermes_agent scripts, L-Fleet coord_pulse cursor-agent jobs — never conflate in skill prose or REGISTRY staging  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_2ecbe4c7eaa6 -->
- hermes-delegate and hermes_harness.py are L-PT parallel AIAgent threads — NOT Nous delegate_task and NOT Hermes-native subagent children  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_1fda0fc21d52 -->
- Windows coord_pulse dispatches cursor-agent via win_job_queue — not Hermes delegate_task; subagent/win-* git branches are L-Fleet file-inbox coordination fiction  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_23b2a4dce4a8 -->
- bin/agents REGISTRY.yml maps OpenClaw pipeline stages to Hermes profile staging — not a live runtime subagent tree  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_0343a2bd15b2 -->
- Scheduled coord_pulse on Windows does not load .env.local — ORAMA_SYSTEM_PATH and PERPETUA_TOOLS_PATH must be User-level env vars or explicit exports  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_5139fb44b427 -->
- Do not run sync-gbrain code stage while gbrain autopilot is active — orchestrator refuses destructive source ops; disable autopilot LaunchAgent first when lock or stale index  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c70ab2e5a60e -->
- OpenClaw to Hermes graft must run Wave 0 taxonomy and lane tags before Wave 1 JSON envelope — SKIP recursive-spawn-protocol into hermes-delegate until rename  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c9fb1689c1aa -->
- Activating a provider config in a tool's own settings store (e.g. Cline's providers.json) does not make the CLI use it at runtime -- check for a separate top-level provider-selection flag/default before assuming a config write took effect  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_49e8701ba9e9 -->
- A vendor-doc's exact sample curl/Python for a third-party OpenAI-compatible API is not guaranteed correct -- an optional-looking parameter (an OpenAI-Project header, or the SDK's project= kwarg) can silently break auth (401) even though the underlying key is valid; test with and without each optional param before trusting the documented sample verbatim  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_3c85823ef46f -->
- When extracting or inspecting a secret from a config file via a shell one-liner, never use a bare grep/cat on the raw file -- pipe through a redaction step or use a script that only reports length/presence, even when the extraction command's PRIMARY purpose looks safe (e.g. checking a var name exists)  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_0c78b132c55b -->
- GNU coreutils flock(1) (the standalone command) is not present on macOS/BSD by default -- confirmed missing even on a fully-set-up dev machine, not just a fresh install. Never gate a bash script's mutual-exclusion on the flock command; use Python's fcntl.flock() instead (a real POSIX syscall wrapper available in Python's stdlib on Darwin) if the script already shells out to python3, or the portable mkdir-is-atomic lock idiom if it's pure bash.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_a10088cab4ae -->
- Second-precision timestamps used as unique filenames (e.g. for rotating backup/version history) will silently collide under any workload faster than 1 call per second -- including two successive calls from the same automated script or an agent iterating quickly. Always use microsecond (or finer) precision, a monotonic counter, or a uuid suffix for filename-based versioning, never bare %Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_7ef854a99c2d -->
- A plan/audit document's own 'Done' status table is not proof the work is actually done for every row -- verify each claimed-complete item against the real filesystem/content individually, the same rigor as verifying an external upstream claim, just scoped internally to this repo's own docs.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_d232d1a1d91d -->
- For any tool that hot-swaps between named configs carrying live credentials (API keys, provider profiles), never write directly into the live config file inline -- always run through: (1) a checked-in *.tmpl with ${VAR} placeholders holding zero real secrets, (2) a resolver that fills placeholders from a canonical secrets/<name> (0600) + .env.<name> loader pair, (3) an idempotent snapshot of the outgoing state into a rotating, capped backup history stored OUTSIDE the git repo (since a snapshot holds a real resolved secret), before the atomic write. This is now the established convention on this machine for every provider secret set up this session (Firecrawl, GitHub Copilot, Cline's Google/wanDB/BigModel profiles) -- follow it for any new one rather than inventing a new scheme.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_322a2fba2337 -->
- When a concurrent agent commits directly to the same working directory/branch mid-review, verify by reading actual diffs and running the actual test suite before crediting or trusting any of its claimed fixes -- never trust a commit message's claimed test count.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_11baf3e69fcb -->
- Independent dual-voice AI review (Codex + a separate Claude subagent, no shared context) that converges on the identical bug by reading the same code independently is the strongest verification signal available -- weight it above either voice's severity rating alone.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_fb76280fc2cd -->
- The EXA daemon-wrapper pattern (singleton daemon + Unix-socket bridge to avoid N duplicate npx child processes) only applies to locally-spawned stdio MCP servers -- it does not transfer to type:http MCP servers like Firecrawl/GitHub Copilot, since Claude Code connects to those directly via HTTP with no local backend process to daemonize.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_2c4124092607 -->
- An agent renaming a directory/binary must never also infer a broader identity change (module path, package namespace, org identity) in the same commit unless explicitly authorized — each identity-bearing change needs its own explicit human sign-off, even when bundled with an authorized structural rename. When citing live go.mod values after such an incident, verify each repository independently: GitHub repo lineage (wesm/agentsview redirects to kenn-io/agentsview) does not imply a fork already adopted the parent vanity module path.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_d7f233fdd47e -->
- Meta-instruction, in effect until the v2 oramasys org multi-repo centralization is complete: before pushing a fix to either orama-system or Perpetua-Tools, check whether the same file, or the same underlying concept, exists in the sibling repo and whether it's actually in sync -- not just "did I apply the fix I meant to apply," but "is the BEST version of this fix now present in both repos, chosen by comparing them directly, not by assuming whichever repo I fixed first is automatically correct." Where the two repos' versions differ, the better one (more defensive pattern, more complete investigation notes, more accurate glob/scope) should be adopted in both, not just the one being actively worked on. This bidirectional check is a stopgap for the current two-repo-with-manual-sync architecture; once v2's centralized package/multi-repo structure lands, this class of drift should become structurally impossible rather than something that needs checking by hand every time.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_b1cfecbc4eb6 -->
- Meta-instruction, in effect until the v2 oramasys org multi-repo centralization is complete: before pushing a fix to either orama-system or Perpetua-Tools, check whether the same file, or the same underlying concept, exists in the sibling repo and whether it's actually in sync -- not just "did I apply the fix I meant to apply," but "is the BEST version of this fix now present in both repos, chosen by comparing them directly, not by assuming whichever repo I fixed first is automatically correct." Where the two repos' versions differ, the better one (more defensive pattern, more complete investigation notes, more accurate glob/scope) should be adopted in both, not just the one being actively worked on. This bidirectional check is a stopgap for the current two-repo-with-manual-sync architecture; once v2's centralized package/multi-repo structure lands, this class of drift should become structurally impossible rather than something that needs checking by hand every time.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_6ee66e3cd922 -->
- When a local episodic/memory branch reports ahead-of-origin by a small N, identify the exact commits, check reachability from main and the active consolidation branch, and compare patch-id/content before assuming unmerged feature work — local closeout memory often sits only on a stale tip after related PRs already merged.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_1103c662e580 -->
- ~~Cherry-picking append-only JSONL memory (AGENT_LEARNINGS.jsonl) onto a divergent tip: resolve EOF conflicts by union/dedup on source.run_id, append only unique rows using the original line bytes, and never json.dumps-rewrite the whole file (Unicode escape churn creates huge false diffs).~~  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_0f50e7407f77 superseded_by=lesson_aca46de3a5aa -->
- Cherry-picking append-only JSONL memory (AGENT_LEARNINGS.jsonl) onto a divergent tip: resolve EOF conflicts by union/dedup on top-level id or nested source.run_id (first occurrence wins per key), append only unique rows using the original line bytes, and never json.dumps-rewrite the whole file (Unicode escape churn creates huge false diffs).  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_aca46de3a5aa -->
- Append-only historical records must never be rewritten in place, even to fix a real error -- including via checkout --theirs or any other whole-file overwrite during conflict resolution. The correct fix: restore the original record exactly, then append a new entry whose supersedes field names the original lesson id, carrying only the corrected claim text. checkout --theirs looks like a clean conflict resolution but is exactly the in-place-mutation this doctrine bans when applied to an append-only record file, not a regular source file.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_9940e1aa6fc4 -->
- Append-only historical records (lessons, audits, vulnerability memory, review ledgers, and any file whose value depends on an intact record of what was actually claimed and when) must never be rewritten in place, even to fix a real error in the original entry. A direct edit destroys the original claim with no queryable trace within the data itself of what it used to say -- exactly the auditability the record exists to provide. The correct fix: append a new entry that supersedes the original (a supersedes/superseded_by back-reference), never mutate the original's fields. This applies to the record itself, not to copies rendered or cached from it -- a machine-generated markdown file regenerated from a corrected append-only source is fine to regenerate in place, and non-historical documents (working docs, prose restating a claim) can be edited directly.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_071ec367227c -->
- ~~git branch -f <name> <sha> silently no-ops (or unreliably applies) when <name> is the currently checked-out branch -- it does not reliably move HEAD in the same shell invocation. Use git update-ref refs/heads/<name> <sha> instead for a scripted undo-a-cherry-pick loop while staying on the branch; it is a lower-level plumbing command that reliably moves the ref and (since HEAD is a symref to it) is immediately reflected.~~  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_2546180f3d5b superseded_by=lesson_e276758511e6 -->
- ~~git branch -f <name> <sha> refuses to move the currently checked-out branch (exits non-zero; the ref is unchanged). Use git update-ref refs/heads/<name> <sha> to move only the ref while staying on the branch; then synchronize index and working tree afterward (for example git restore --staged --worktree .) so staged/worktree state does not still reflect the undone cherry-pick.~~  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_e276758511e6 superseded_by=lesson_32aea4e6cfff -->
- git restore --staged --worktree . discards ALL unrelated staged and unstaged changes in the working tree, not just the undone cherry-pick's files -- it is a destructive broad-reset disguised as cleanup. Before running it, require a clean git status --short (verifying nothing unrelated is dirty), or scope the restore to only the specific paths the undone commit touched (git restore --staged --worktree -- <path>...). The git update-ref refs/heads/<name> <sha> guidance itself remains correct and unaffected.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_32aea4e6cfff -->
- A subprocess worker pool that only calls proc.kill() on timeout leaves grandchild processes running: kill() signals the direct python -c child only, not any process it spawns itself. Fix: launch each worker with start_new_session=True (own process group), then on timeout send os.killpg(pid, SIGTERM) first (let it clean up), escalate to os.killpg(pid, SIGKILL) if it doesn't exit within a few seconds. Separately, redirect worker stdout/stderr to temp files instead of subprocess.PIPE -- a chatty worker writing more than the OS pipe buffer (~64KB) deadlocks against a poll loop that only drains pipes after the worker exits; files never fill up under the writer, and CLAYGO cleanup (os.remove after reading) keeps them from accumulating.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_92d985d17f3d -->
- An aggregate counter over an append-only JSONL lessons store (e.g. a dashboard's accepted-count) must not do a naive per-line status==accepted count -- retract_lesson.py appends a NEW row with the SAME id and status=retracted rather than editing the original in place, so the original accepted row is still on disk and gets double-counted forever unless the counter dedupes by id (latest row wins). Separately, supersession creates a DIFFERENT id whose supersedes field points at the old one, and the old id's row still literally has status=accepted -- so counters also need to exclude any id referenced by another accepted lesson's supersedes field, mirroring render_lessons.py's _build_auto_section pattern (only ACCEPTED supersessions retire the old lesson; a provisional supersede must not).  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_67383a83bee9 -->
- A repo-wide banned-term grep in a policy doc (e.g. reject unauthorized use of a reserved word like coordinator) false-positives on vendored dependency code that happens to contain the same substring for unrelated reasons -- e.g. a Python virtualenv's bundled pygments lexer listing Apple Cocoa API class names ending in Coordinator is not a policy violation. Fix: scope the check to git ls-files (tracked files only, excluding untracked dependency directories by construction) instead of a plain recursive grep with manually maintained exclude flags -- more robust because it can't be defeated by a new dependency directory the exclude list doesn't yet know about.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_9f3386cb6274 -->
- Even with file-disjoint parallel agent dispatch (each job assigned a distinct set of files), a follow-up direct edit to a file that job is still actively refining can race: reading the file's tail output once and then Edit-ing it later based on that stale read can silently merge on top of content the dispatched job wrote AFTER your read, producing a coherent-looking but unverified final state. The Edit tool's own warning (file changed on disk since last read) is the signal to stop and re-Read fresh before trusting the diff, not just proceed because the edit applied without a hard conflict.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_4e38d15d83ad -->
- When a stacked-PR-family branch's earlier commits have already landed upstream via a different merge (e.g. a sibling PR's squash-merge), git rebase origin/master auto-detects patch-content equivalence and drops the now-redundant commits, printing 'dropping <sha> ... -- patch contents already upstream'; treat that message as confirmation of successful recovery, not an error, and use rebase as the recovery mechanic instead of manual cherry-pick surgery.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_15aa463fd07c -->
- ~~No blanket 'always prefer ours' or 'always prefer theirs' rule is correct for a 3-way merge conflict — read and understand both sides' actual intent and judge case-by-case by which version is the strict quality/robustness superset of the other.~~  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_005f2a16600d superseded_by=lesson_2f377d3a025a -->
- git merge-file -p ours base theirs can literally duplicate an identical textual addition when both 'ours' and 'theirs' independently added the exact same content at slightly different anchor points relative to 'base' -- inspect merge-tool preview output for back-to-back duplicate blocks before promoting or applying it; re-running an apply step that rebuilds the preview directory from scratch wipes any manual de-duplication, so it must be redone if the target sha changes.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_05c055046864 -->
- A merge or blend tool's own 'clean, no conflict' label for a file is not proof nothing was silently dropped -- independently diff base-commit..target-commit for every file the tool marks clean, not just the ones it flags as conflicts, to positively confirm upstream made zero changes there.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_70713965dc1b -->
- When temporarily repointing a git submodule's .gitmodules url at your own fork to consume not-yet-merged upstream PRs, document the temporary state and exact revert path in at least 3 places -- an inline comment in .gitmodules itself, the vendor-tracking doc's current-pin section, and any blend-state or catalog file's note field -- and validate end-to-end via a real fresh-clone plus git submodule update --init, not just the url change alone.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_0f66a36ee1cc -->
- A 'walk this directory and copy or record everything' tool (e.g. a blend-promote script's find $DIR -type f) must explicitly exclude build and bytecode-cache artifacts like __pycache__ compiled files -- .gitignore protects git commits but not tool-level file-walk bookkeeping that reads the raw filesystem directly, so a stray verification command's side-effect files can get silently copied and catalogued as if they were real content.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_76cbf68d38b1 -->
- A GitHub PR's --base must name a branch that exists on the base/target repo, never a fork-only branch -- gh pr create --base <fork-only-branch> against an upstream repo fails with 'Base ref must be a branch' or 'No commits between'; for a family of PRs meant to stack across a fork boundary, base every PR on the upstream default branch (e.g. master) and communicate the dependency in prose (PR body or comments), not via GitHub's native base-branch stacking.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_fcf6fe38c82e -->
- No blanket always-prefer-ours or always-prefer-theirs rule is correct for a 3-way merge conflict — read both sides and prefer the strict quality/robustness superset when one exists; when neither side dominates, combine compatible changes and stop for human review on remaining conflicts so unique valid content is not discarded.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_2f377d3a025a -->
- Never trust a cherry -v + commit-message match as proof a merge/reanchor preserved content — always diff the local tip against the actual merged headRefOid before declaring it safe  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_adda4d2b02c3 -->
- When a CodeRabbit finding cites a language-agnostic coding-style guideline (e.g. '**/*: always create new objects, use immutable update patterns'), verify the pattern has a real non-degrading equivalent in the target language before applying it, rather than mechanically transferring it.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_022af4a9d2b8 -->
- After fixing a process-substitution exit-status blind spot (a producer wrapped in '< <(...)' whose failure a while-read loop can't see), check every remaining stage of that same data flow for the identical gap -- a partial fix that only materializes the FIRST producer to a file can still leave a SECOND filter stage (awk, a second python3 call) wrapped in its own swallowing process substitution.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_189690ff2855 -->
- When a git-worktree cleanup path falls back to plain recursive delete because the proper removal command failed, that fallback does not clear git's internal worktree admin registration -- only the dedicated prune command does; omitting it leaves stale worktree/detached-HEAD metadata that accumulates across every failed removal.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_774d6e8ca9a9 -->
- When cherry-reanchoring a stale branch onto a rewritten/advanced main and hitting an add/add or content conflict, don't resolve by reflexively picking a side -- check whether origin/main's CURRENT full file already contains the branch's content (possibly refined, redacted, or relocated), whether the same lesson/memory IDs already exist on main with equal-or-better text, and for gitlink/submodule conflicts, which SHA is actually ahead. A resolution of 'take origin/main entirely, cherry-pick becomes empty' is a common, CORRECT outcome for old branches, not a failed reconciliation.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_a4a16527bbd1 -->
- During branch reanchor or merge conflict resolution, if one side has a real literal (LAN IP, workstation path, personal email used as an infra detail) and the other side has a placeholder or redacted form of the same value, always keep the redacted side regardless of which side is otherwise 'newer' or 'more detailed' -- a security redaction must never be silently reverted by a content merge.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_1888719f8a52 -->
- The same clustering-before-shipping discipline recurs at five zoom levels and should be applied consistently at all of them: (1) one coherent fix per file, (2) group fixes into commits by theme not touch-order, (3) batch all logical commits locally before a single push, (4) stay on one open branch/PR rather than fragmenting across new ones unless none exists yet, (5) at repo-architecture scale, decide per-function whether it earns its own top-level repo or stays a docs/v2/ subsection of a shared repo -- the same 'does this deserve its own container' judgment, just at a bigger radius.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_442afa34388a -->
- Before any cross-repo guard-sync operation (or when a user asks 'is the other side actually behind, or does it have unique improvements?'), run the divergence checker proactively even when confident nothing has diverged -- treat empirical verification as the mandatory first step, not a defensive measure reserved for when drift is already suspected.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_fa9f7eac575e -->
- check-guard-sync-divergence.sh's WORKSPACE_ROOT defaults to the parent directory of the canonical repo root -- when the canonical repo being pushed from is itself a disposable/scratch clone sitting directly under /tmp (a common pattern for isolating risky git-history-surgery operations), this default silently expands the scan to every unrelated scratch clone under /tmp, producing false-positive GUARD_SYNC_E_DIVERGENCE blocks on totally unrelated pushes. Fix: run the push from a workspace directory that has no other sibling git repos (move/clone the canonical repo into its own isolated parent dir), or verify the flagged 'siblings' are genuinely unrelated scratch work before treating the block as blocking.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c15cb89de6d4 -->
- Reconciliation decisions (which side of a conflict, divergence, or finding is correct) should be made from what's actually verified to be there, not from which side looks more current by a fast heuristic (recency, version number, 'the other side is usually just behind'). The heuristic and the verified answer usually agree, which is exactly what makes skipping verification feel safe -- the cases where they diverge are silent regressions with no error message anywhere to catch them later.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_8aa89cff9199 -->
- When two independent branches/sessions both attempt to fix the same underlying rule/bug and produce textually different diffs, don't assume they conflict or that one supersedes the other -- install the actual tool (semgrep, in this case) and empirically test both against real positive/negative samples before judging. Two genuinely different-looking fixes can both be correct (same bug, convergent solutions), which is a stronger signal than either fix alone.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_31a635516ea1 -->
- semgrep Bash language support (marked experimental by semgrep's own blog) does not reliably expose quoted strings as a distinct string-literal AST node -- a bare metavariable captures a quoted value INCLUDING its surrounding quotes as part of a generic expression match, while the literal-metavariable quoted syntax (meant to match string literals and strip the quotes) silently matches nothing at all against real bash string assignments. Before trusting any bash-language semgrep or opengrep rule that uses that literal-metavariable quoted syntax or a nested metavariable-pattern block without an explicit generic-language override, test it empirically against real sample code -- a rule that looks syntactically correct can be a silent no-op.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_e8d8ee140589 -->
- When a bash script pipes an external tool through '2>/dev/null || true' inside a scan loop, a missing binary (not just a missing match) silently produces zero hits — always guard with 'command -v <tool>' before the loop, and never trust an environment-isolation fix as root cause until CI itself confirms it against a captured execution trace  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_90fcf80b1355 -->
- Treat ECC upstream advancement and local-overlay restoration as separate idempotent steps: verify the upstream SHA, accept a clean reviewed checkout, restore every approved patch path, and report success only after restoration.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_4e6559b2a159 -->
- When a reviewed patch is guarded by byte equality, generate it with full blob IDs so Git core.abbrev cannot turn identical overlay content into false unreviewed drift.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_538dbf2d15ce -->
- Review depth is not review diversity: N voices inside one orchestration correlate far more than their independence suggests, so treat in-harness cross-model agreement as weak evidence and get at least one cold, out-of-harness reviewer before calling a plan reviewed.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_3630a43dbd40 -->
- A fix needs its own review: closing a finding is a change like any other and can be wrong in exactly the way the original defect was, so re-review corrections rather than marking the finding closed on the strength of having written a fix.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_5537a6a2cbeb -->
- Archive-first is not rollback-safe on its own: if the source is cleared after a successful archive and every activation path then fails, the backup exists while the live artifact is gone, so stage and validate the replacement before switching, prefer atomic rename, and otherwise restore the archived source before raising.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_5393b0709b12 -->
- Posting a status note to the GossipBus board does not refresh agent liveness: only heartbeat pulse writes the agent_pulse rows the liveness roster reads, so an agent can be loudly visible on the board and simultaneously counted DEAD - pulse your own identity on a cadence tighter than the 30 minute threshold.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_4fc2c15f4073 -->
- Never make a board write path auto-refresh liveness for whatever identity it is handed: agent_coordination.py log accepts an arbitrary agent_id, so auto-pulsing on log would let any session refresh any other identity and fake liveness - fix roster staleness on the read side instead, and never prune historical registrations.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_5260d66fd687 -->
- Agent capabilities fail independently: a CLI can be unreliable for unattended headless tool use and still be a strong analysis and review voice, so scope a degraded agent to the role it can do rather than benching it entirely.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_2542124bca2f -->
- A workflow agent killed by a session usage limit can leave real, uncommitted work in the tree: always run git status and salvage-commit before relaunching, because a naive retry silently destroys it.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c840e366a05d -->
- When a Claude session budget is the binding constraint, route implementation to codex exec with workspace-write scoped to the worktree and keep Claude seats as thin supervisors that verify the diff and own the commit: the quota is independent and the sandbox scope doubles as a safety guardrail.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_59e4c409926a -->
- For web-based agents in an ephemeral command sandbox, the filesystem can disappear between turns while conversation context survives. Commit and push each independently verified unit promptly; after a reset, re-clone and re-verify tools, files, and tests from scratch while continuing on the same branch name. This recovery discipline differs from persistent local command-line sessions and does not relax test-first verification.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_df682761347f -->
- Perpetua-Tools contains TS/JS packages (packages/alphaclaw-adapter/, packages/alphaclaw-mcp/) whose stated purpose is to steer/commandeer AlphaClaw -- but this is a runtime-only relationship, never a repo-level one, and matters for scope decisions under a standing "never touch AlphaClaw's repo" constraint. Verified directly: these packages spawn a local AlphaClaw process (child_process.spawn("node", ["bin/alphaclaw.js", "start"])) and communicate with it over plain HTTP (http.request) once it's running. There is no git clone, checkout, commit, or any other git operation against diazMelgarejo/AlphaClaw anywhere in this code -- it assumes AlphaClaw is already installed/checked out as a sibling and only ever controls the running process and its HTTP surface. Code sharing AlphaClaw's name, or referencing its branches (e.g. a comment naming feature/MacOS-post-install) by way of explaining what it talks to, is not the same as touching AlphaClaw's repository -- this PT-owned code is in scope for PT-side work even when the broader task explicitly excludes AlphaClaw itself.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_a5b40efe18d5 -->
- Supersedes only the adapter-specific portion of the older D1 versioning decision by explicit human override. An adapter package version must track the real upstream release lineage of its integration branch, use valid semantic versioning, and make a small lineage-based increment; do not jump to an unrelated round number. This package change does not alter the separate external integration branch.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_9bcca08eefa4 -->
- When an unsafe endpoint override falls back to an environment-derived host, validate and reconstruct the fallback through the same endpoint-policy boundary; otherwise rejected metadata-service destinations can re-enter through the recovery path.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_4195346d48ca -->
- For shared-repository board jobs, invoke the supported coordination CLI from its owning repository, then verify the claimed source revision against the actual worktree before review; agents must self-register and emit heartbeat pulses, and a claim or log entry is not evidence of task completion.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_a41e85086a8c -->
- A passing focused pytest selection is insufficient as a completion gate when duplicate test function names can shadow earlier cases; require unique test definitions and record whether the repository-wide required gate passed, failed on baseline debt, or was not run.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_5061e28c03ab -->
- Treat .claude/hooks/.logs/hook-log.jsonl as transient runtime telemetry, never as a reviewed ECC overlay: the autoresearch hook helper appends per-session records under its current working directory. Preserve hook behavior and tests as the durable artifact; keep generated evidence local or in a deliberate audit system, and exclude it from submodule overlay candidates.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_afda999fe3d9 -->
- Use regular commits and merge commits for normal integration; reserve squash merges, history rewrites, and expunges for explicit human-approved privacy, security, or faulty-history remediation.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_f11f751d7446 -->
- Before changing a repository's mirrored hook or guard file to satisfy a broad repair request, first inspect the synchronization manifest. Files designated as canonical-managed must be changed in their source repository, merged normally, and then synchronized to each mirror; a request for one branch does not authorize a downstream fork of shared infrastructure.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_20234c4410fd -->
- When a shell script resolves its own location through its source path for self-referential logic, a test that only changes the subprocess working directory while invoking the real on-disk script does not exercise a different-checkout scenario. Copy the script and its sourced dependencies into the synthetic fixture, then invoke that copy.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_fb41758b35af -->
- A negative assertion in a subprocess-based hook test can pass vacuously when the process exits early for an unrelated reason. Pair it with an exact successful return-code assertion so the test proves the intended code path ran.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_9c02865055b4 -->
- ~~When an unsafe endpoint override falls back to environment-derived metadata, validate and reconstruct the fallback through the same endpoint-policy boundary before use.~~  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_b1324b9d0481 superseded_by=lesson_4195346d48ca -->
- When endpoint-policy exceptions can retain raw addresses or URLs, logs and stderr must emit only redacted fields rather than exception text or raw endpoint values.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_d7842688fbb3 -->
- When a script compares sibling repositories by directory, linked worktrees sharing one Git common directory are alternate checkouts of the same authority, not independent mirror targets. Resolve and compare their common-directory paths, then skip same-repository worktrees rather than reporting divergence.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_ecf446018e17 -->
- A script that runs git against another repository from a hook or hook call chain must clear Git's local-environment variables before the first cross-repository call. Ask Git for the variable list and unset each value; otherwise inherited context can silently redirect a target-repository command back to the invoking repository.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_e6771ac33caa -->
- When multiple sessions concurrently change infrastructure-critical hooks or guard scripts, use one append-only coordination board. Record status before and after consequential steps, reread the board before acting, and treat the latest timestamped ruling as authoritative when live verification changes an earlier understanding.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_f5a3139114c2 -->
- PR body updates are append-only — ManagePullRequest update_pr and gh pr edit replace the entire body. After every commit on a branch with an open PR, run bash scripts/git/remind-pr-body-append-only.sh before push; use scripts/cursor/append-pr-body.sh for writes. Documented clobber incidents: PT#154, orama#222, PT#298, orama#239, PT#314; treat ~5x undocumented silent clobbers as likely. PR_BODY_GUARD_STRICT=1 blocks publish until PR_BODY_UPDATE_ACK=1.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_59b1441fc3a8 -->
- When resolving real overlap between a new draft and an existing, more comprehensive doctrine, "avoid duplication" does not automatically mean "make the newer, simpler thing subordinate to the older, more complex one." It means determining which document actually serves which situation and sizing each to its own job. A heavyweight protocol built for a rare, hard problem (e.g. concurrent multi-agent edits to the same repo, or reconciling a fork separated from its source by months of drift) should not become the default an agent reads first for the common, simple case (one branch, one agent, no concurrent editing) just because it existed first and is more thorough. Getting this backwards is not caught by re-reading a project's own stated single-source-of-truth value, since that value is genuinely being honored in some sense (no literal duplication) even while the sizing is wrong -- it may require direct correction from a human collaborator who can see the actual audience mismatch.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_82681579036e -->
- Several tooling and API gotchas recur across a working session even after being individually caught once, because a single encounter doesn't automatically generalize into a standing rule: the PR-list API's merged field being unreliable (must check the single-PR endpoint); squash-merged branches never showing as git ancestors (verify by content/ID presence, not ancestry); a stale local checkout after an earlier push in the same session diverging silently; and diff-scoped CI lint checking a whole touched file, not just the changed hunk. Each of these needs to be treated as a standing checklist item to consult before the relevant operation, not a one-off lesson learned and then re-derived from scratch the next time it's encountered.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_da0f36431b4d -->
- When syncing attribution guards across PT, orama, and AlphaClaw, fix PT on its single open guard PR first, then byte-copy only GUARD_SYNC manifest paths to the one open PR per downstream repo — never open fragmented one-file PRs and never run orama-to-PT sync until PT canonical merges, or stale orama copies overwrite PT improvements.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_bb59b8b3866b -->
- Before running sync-attribution-guard-scripts.sh, run check-guard-sync-divergence.sh --workspace; abort when any sibling guard file differs from orama HEAD and the sibling blob is absent from orama history — that means the sibling is ahead and blind sync would clobber improvements. Promote sibling to orama on the single open PR per repo, then sync downstream.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_941c137e676d -->
- Layer 0 for Cursor agents: never automatically change an existing PR description — use ManagePullRequest post_comment or gh pr comment only; hooks block update_pr with body=, gh pr edit, and append-pr-body.sh  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_16a21ff0e36b -->
- Guard-sync waves use one open PR per repo (PT then orama then AlphaClaw); run check-guard-sync-divergence.sh --workspace before sync-attribution-guard-scripts.sh; never open parallel one-file guard PRs  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_5c3d89dbc279 -->
- Cloud turn-end update PR must mean post_comment not update_pr body — treating description field as a scratch pad erases Summary template sections and CodeRabbit tails  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_d316b0fc442a -->
- When markdownlint-cli2 v0.17.2 lints explicit file globs in CI, root .markdownlint.json filter overrides do not apply to catalog SKILL.md files outside the skills tree — add a per-directory .markdownlint-cli2.jsonc (e.g. line_length 500 for bin/orama-system/) and delete conflicting .markdownlint.json.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_1a53b1646fc6 -->
- Guard-sync divergence checks must discover workspace siblings via git -C path rev-parse --show-toplevel (not test -d path/.git), reject surplus CLI arguments with exit 2, fail-closed when check-guard-sync-divergence.sh is missing, and scan the full outgoing pre-push commit range for scripts/git/ touches.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_21059dd8052f -->
- PR body Layer 0: Cursor agents comment only (post_comment or gh pr comment). Body writes require an operator TTY grant via grant-pr-body-human-override.sh; only append-pr-body.sh is allowed after grant — never update_pr body=, gh pr edit, gh api body mutations, or agent-forgeable env exports.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_511a1712b21d -->
- PR-body operator grant v2 replay state machine: append-pr-body.sh must reserve nonce before gh pr edit, mark-applied only after remote success, consume last; release reservation on edit failure; reconcile CLI consumes when follow-up block already on remote (crash recovery). Never consume before gh pr edit succeeds.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_d10e35a545c6 -->
- operator-grant-v2 HMAC canonical payload is fixed-order UTF-8 bytes grant-v2|repo|pr|nonce|issued_at|action|content_digest with no trailing newline; mint and verify must use identical bytes; golden vector test locks regression.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_a518b75fffcd -->
- Same-user Keychain HMAC for PR-body grants is escalation control not cryptographic human identity — agent shells with PTY can read the secret or ack file; MVP raises effort; WebAuthn deferred to security-sentinel v2.1.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_9b75082708c0 -->
- Operator-gated PR body edits require operator-grant-v2 HMAC ack from grant-pr-body-human-override.sh matching append bytes -- not CURSOR_PR_BODY_HUMAN_OVERRIDE_ACK env exports or operator-grant-v1 plaintext ack. The HMAC grant authorizes the append-pr-body.sh capability itself; it does not prove human identity or presence -- the underlying grant script's TTY-presence check is not a reliable human-vs-agent signal.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_edd577b3ccc6 -->
- Tracked .agent memory and working chronicles must not embed ephemeral scratch path literals — repo_hygiene flags them like workstation home paths and blocks PT CI.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_68db9159860d -->
- When a markdownlint (or similar whole-file linter) violation surfaces in a file pulled into scope by an unrelated edit, the correct response depends on the file's nature, not a blanket policy: machine-rendered, append-only historical records (a lessons/decisions log where entries are added mechanically over time, meant to be terse and grow indefinitely) should be excluded from the specific rule that doesn't meaningfully apply (e.g. line-length on a log that append-only tooling writes one dense entry per line), via a scoped config override -- not hand-reformatted, since doing so fights the tool that generates the file. Genuinely hand-authored prose (a narrative doc, a plan, a README) should have violations fixed properly, even if the count is nontrivial, since the rule is actually serving its purpose there. Determine which kind of file it is by reading its actual content and structure (does it read as authored narrative, or as accumulated terse entries with a consistent machine-written shape) before choosing exclude vs. fix, not by file location or extension alone.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_7d90ebd049d0 -->
- When a code review lists multiple findings, checking each one against the actual current file content before fixing it is not a formality -- in a real remediation pass, roughly half of a review's findings can already be correctly handled in current code (fixed by an earlier commit, or never actually present as described), and treating every finding as needing action wastes effort and risks introducing unnecessary or even incorrect changes to code that was already right. The check itself is cheap (grep or read the exact lines cited); skipping it in favor of blindly applying every suggested diff is the expensive mistake.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_1d6547afedda -->
- A security guard that inspects shell commands by splitting on command separators (&&, ;, ||) to check each resulting segment independently must also split on bare newlines -- a shell interprets a newline as a command separator too, and a guard that omits it leaves an exploitable gap: a legitimate-looking command on the first line can cause the guard to short-circuit to ALLOW, leaving a malicious second command hidden on the next line completely uninspected. When testing such a guard's segment-splitting logic, the test must exercise the actual bypass condition (the legitimate first command's branch must be genuinely capable of returning ALLOW, e.g. via a properly-mocked active authorization state) -- a test where the first segment already denies on its own passes regardless of whether the fix is present, proving nothing about the real vulnerability.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_1786f0d5765e -->
- A conditional guard like `if [ -f "$script" ] && command -v "$interpreter" >/dev/null; then run "$script"; else run_fallback; fi` silently downgrades security coverage whenever the interpreter is missing but the primary script exists -- it falls through to a narrower fallback path without any signal that the stronger check was skipped. The fallback branch should be reserved for the case the comment actually describes (the primary script itself being absent, e.g. an old clone that predates it); a present-but-unrunnable primary script should fail loudly and closed, not silently downgrade to a weaker check.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_1777826b29c2 -->
- When a config fix for a CI tool (linter, scanner, formatter) is verified locally before pushing, verifying against a locally-installed "latest" or default version of that tool is not sufficient -- CI workflows commonly pin a specific action/tool version (e.g. a GitHub Action tagged @v19) that can be substantially older than whatever installs by default locally, and newer tool versions can silently support config schema or CLI flags that the older, actually-pinned version does not. A config change that tests clean locally can still fail in real CI for this reason alone. Before trusting a local verification of a CI-tool config fix, identify the exact tool version the CI workflow actually pins (check the action's own release notes or its own bundled dependency versions, often visible in error message doc-links CI itself produces) and install that exact version locally to test against, not the newest available.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_3946263acde6 -->
- Secret-scanning tools like gitleaks scan the full commit history range in a branch/PR by default, not just the current tree state -- a test fixture or example value that merely resembles a secret (e.g. a hardcoded hex digest used as an expected HMAC output) can trip a generic entropy detector, and even after a later commit fixes the pattern by removing the literal value, the scanner keeps flagging the earlier, now-superseded commit on every subsequent CI run for that branch, because the flagged content is still present in that commit's diff within history. This is a real, recurring category of false positive distinct from an actual leaked secret, and the correct fix is a scoped allowlist entry for the specific commit SHA(s) in the scanner's config (with `useDefault = true` or equivalent to keep the rest of the default ruleset intact) -- not editing the current file again, since the flagged content is no longer there, and not disabling the scanner or its ruleset broadly.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_07d06982cc21 -->
- A short/abbreviated git commit SHA (e.g. from `git log --oneline` output) must never be extended into a full 40-character SHA by guessing, padding, or pattern-completing the remaining hex digits -- even when the extension is meant only for a config file (an allowlist, a pin) rather than executable code, a fabricated SHA silently fails to match anything (a security tool's allowlist entry that doesn't match the real commit provides zero actual exemption, while looking correct at a glance) or, worse, could coincidentally collide with an unrelated real commit. Always resolve a full SHA explicitly with `git rev-parse <short-sha>` and use that exact output, verified by direct comparison, before writing it into any tracked file.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_25e9a6680295 -->
- When two changes bundled in the same commit are meant to work together (a feature-gating flag and the feature it gates, a deferral/rollout date and the behavior that respects it), verify they are actually consistent with each other by running the specific test that exercises that consistency together -- not just trusting each change's own isolated test coverage. A change that extends or modifies a gate without checking what depends on that gate's current state can silently ship an internal contradiction that only surfaces once something exercises both halves at once.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_e9851c227da9 -->
- When removing a duplicate call site as a concurrency/correctness fix, check whether that call site actually serves two different purposes depending on which code state it runs in before deleting it everywhere -- the same fallback or retry call can be genuine duplication in one state (e.g. a single-racer scenario where the fallback target is already the racer that just failed) and the intended, necessary behavior in a different state (e.g. a multi-racer scenario where the fallback target was never itself one of the racers). Removing it unconditionally fixes the duplication bug in the first state while introducing a regression in the second; the correct fix is usually conditional on which state actually applies, not a blanket removal.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_1531cec802aa -->
- Confirming a large batch of test failures is pre-existing and unrelated to a session's own changes (via a before/after comparison) is a necessary check but not the same as understanding why the failures exist -- stopping there leaves a large, alarming-looking failure count in the record with no actual explanation. Where time allows, finishing the diagnosis by installing missing dependencies one at a time and re-running (watching the count drop) can turn "pre-existing, unrelated, unexplained" into "pre-existing, unrelated, and here is exactly why, with zero project-level fix needed" -- a materially more useful record for whoever encounters the same large failure count next and would otherwise have to re-investigate it from scratch.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_77b7607756d8 -->
- Windows Task Scheduler rejects [TimeSpan]::MaxValue as a RepetitionDuration - it serializes to P99999999DT23H59M59S which the XML schema rejects  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_dfa164663bef -->
- Hermes user-local skills live under HERMES_HOME/skills/<category>/<name>/SKILL.md, not ~/.hermes/skills - HERMES_HOME can be a custom env var pointing elsewhere on Windows  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_febe44b71998 -->
- When a git branch has diverged from origin with unrelated concurrent commits, reset --hard to origin tip and re-apply only your own deltas as fresh edits, rather than fighting an interactive rebase across duplicate/superseded history  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_baff8a16cb91 -->
- A file showing 'M' in git status/diff with every line changed but zero semantic diff (confirmed via diff with tr -d carriage-return) is EOL-attribute false-dirty, not a real edit - fix once with git add <file> to renormalize, never git checkout-- which can silently discard real changes if you're wrong  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_5ada9d1edc15 -->
- PT .agent memory pipeline had unencoded open() calls scattered across graduate.py, render_lessons.py, learn.py beyond the two files fixed earlier (promote.py, auto_dream.py) - the UTF-8 codec bug on Windows cp1252 recurs at every new read site until the whole call chain is audited, not just the entry point  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_004fcd19b31e -->
- scripts/ensure_requirements.ps1 (PowerShell) must stay PowerShell 5.1 compatible on Windows: no ?? null-coalescing operator, no 3+ positional-arg Join-Path calls, and UTF-8 files with em-dashes in double-quoted strings need a BOM  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_79dffce10052 -->
- PowerShell's Set-Content -Encoding UTF8 writes a UTF-8 BOM. When Python reads that same file with open(path, encoding='utf-8') or Path.read_text(encoding='utf-8'), json.loads silently fails on the BOM byte and callers that catch the exception broadly (returning a default like paused=False) get a wrong answer with no visible error - use encoding='utf-8-sig' when reading any file a PowerShell script wrote with Set-Content -Encoding UTF8  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_565585947454 -->
- Before deleting any uncommitted untracked file discovered mid-session as a suspected side-effect, check git log --all -- <path> and cross-reference against other files that import/reference/test it (e.g. a matching tests/test_X.py) - an untracked .py file with a matching test suite is very likely another agent's real in-progress work, not build noise, even if it appeared alongside genuine bootstrap-script side effects in the same git status  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_ddbfab6ad95f -->
- ClinePass AutoResearcher fallback (scripts/cline_autoresearcher.py in orama-system) + parallel dispatch race (_dispatch_race in spawn_agents.py) + solo-mode resilience (lan_peer_session.py, 10x-fail -> demote -> 15min recheck) were ALL already implemented and merged to orama main before this session started work on them — verified via git log + live status check (solo_mode:true, failure_count:65, retry_seconds:900) rather than re-implemented from scratch.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_56c1e184fa2a -->
- A run_in_background git push reporting 'completed (exit code 0)' does NOT prove main is clean or the remote matches — the exit code can come from an early stage of a chained command while a later 'git pull --rebase' or 'git commit' stalls interactively (e.g. mid-rebase waiting on rebase --continue, or a corrupted commit-msg hook). Always follow a backgrounded push with an explicit 'git status --short --branch' check before reporting sync as done.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_71bdc83aa285 -->
- A whole-file git conflict (markers spanning nearly the entire file) usually means one side substantially rewrote the file while the other commit never intended to touch it at all — check 'git show <incoming-commit> -- <file>' first; if the incoming commit's own diff is empty for that file, its tree snapshot is just stale carry-through, and the correct resolution is almost always --ours (the side with the real, intentional changes), not a line-by-line merge.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_57b9cd00f0f5 -->
- When 'git rebase --continue' repeats 'You must edit all merge conflicts and then mark them as resolved using git add' even though git status shows zero unmerged paths and 'git ls-files -u' is empty, the real blocker is downstream in a git hook (commit-msg/pre-commit) failing silently — run 'git commit --no-edit' directly to surface the hook's actual stderr instead of re-reading the stale rebase message.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_50006da8de63 -->
- MCP singleton pattern: when N registrations point to the same server, run ONE backend daemon on a Unix socket and have each registration wrapper probe the socket, start the daemon (nohup, detached) if dead, then bridge its own stdio via Python threading. No socat dependency.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_6be9c514b569 -->
- JSON-RPC multiplexing over a single stdio backend: mangle request IDs to '{client_id}:{original_id}' before forwarding; on responses, split by ':' to demux back to the originating client. Cache initialize capabilities and tools/list results so subsequent clients are served from cache without hitting the backend.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_593789d50e05 -->
- LINT-006 + .mcp.json args: JSON does not expand shell variables, so a literal $HOME in a JSON args array is NOT expanded by the MCP launcher. To avoid committing $HOME/... paths, use bash -c indirection: ["bash","-c","exec bash \"$HOME/path/to/wrapper.sh\""]. The outer bash -c spawns with a real shell that expands $HOME before exec-ing the target.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_a3ecd5d33304 -->
- MCP daemon startup: after launching a background daemon (nohup, detached), probe the Unix socket in a loop with 0.1s sleeps and a hard ceiling (50 iterations = 5s). Fail with a JSON-RPC error response to stderr if the socket never becomes connectable. Do not block stdin or use sleep chains.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_a9e01a372d71 -->
- .agent/ root is NOT a drop zone for arbitrary files. The only recognized subdirectories are memory/, skills/, protocols/, tools/. Policy contracts belong in protocols/; lessons must go through learn.py -> graduate.py -> render pipeline into memory/semantic/LESSONS.md — never as a hand-edited .agent/lessons.md at root.
