(2) Exit codes drive fallback: 0=success, 7/28/52=try next. (3) Setup is idempotent: setup-glm52.sh adds sourcing to shell profiles once; re-runs are safe no-ops. (4) Status visible at startup: every orchestrator run prints whether GLM-5.2 is ACTIVE or NOT AVAILABLE. (5) Operators always know fallback state; silent failures are prevented; diagnostics are available via shell history and endpoint testing.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_a9a1dc2b20d4 -->
- ADR-045: gstack/gbrain/CRG Error Resilience & Safe Defaults. Core framework: (1) Pre-flight error detection (_detect_errors checks CLI, engine status, autopilot jam, stale locks, concurrent writes). (2) Error prevention (timeout guards GBRAIN_TIMEOUT=120/GIT_TIMEOUT=30, retry with exponential backoff 1s→2s→4s, file-based locks for state safety). (3) Error handling (exit code 0/1/7/8/124/127 with recovery strategies). (4) Error messages (use _err_actionable for root cause + actionable fix). (5) Logging (central ~/.openclaw/logs/gstack-gbrain-crg.log). (6) Health checks (post-operation verification). All patterns in bin/orama-system/scripts/lib/gstack-gbrain-crg-safe.sh.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_94210dae95a8 -->
- Implementation Guide for ADR-045: Step-by-step how to harden any orama-system skill using gstack/gbrain/CRG. (1) Identify skills (any that call gbrain/gstack/CRG tools). (2) Add safety library preamble (source bin/orama-system/scripts/lib/gstack-gbrain-crg-safe.sh). (3) Run pre-flight checks (_detect_errors) before first external call. (4) Guard external calls: simple=_retry_with_backoff, state-modifying=_with_lock, queries=timeout+fallback. (5) Add post-operation health checks (_health_check). (6) Use _err_actionable for clear error messages. Priority skills to update: code-review, mcp-install, using-git-worktrees, first-run-setup, orama-gstack. Testing scenarios: happy path, gbrain offline, lock contention, timeout. Checklist in docs/how-to/hardening-gstack-gbrain-skills.md.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_3c20a6f37e9a -->
- gstack-gbrain-crg-safe.sh library functions (6 phases): (1) _detect_errors: pre-flight validation (returns 0 if pass, 1 if fatal). (2) _retry_with_backoff: exponential backoff (1s→2s→4s, max 3 attempts). (3) _with_lock: file-based locking to prevent concurrent writes (returns 8 if lock contention). (4) _handle_error: interpret exit codes (0=ok, 7/124=timeout, 8=lock, 127=not-found). (5) _err_actionable: developer-friendly error output (symptom + root cause + fix). (6) _log_diagnostic: central logging to ~/.openclaw/logs/gstack-gbrain-crg.log. Health check (_health_check) verifies post-operation state. All functions exported for sourcing by skills.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_53550032372d -->
- Fable-5 v1 audit complete: 7 key patterns extracted, all applied to session 2026-07-04 infrastructure work (ADR-045, OpenRouter fallback, startup hardening). v2 blockers cleared. Infrastructure foundation ready for v2 multi-agent workflows.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_082ec4b268a9 -->
- OpenClaw Gateway critical configuration resolved: (1) PT_DIR properly set in workspace+system, (2) all services running (PT:8000, orama:8001, Portal:8002), (3) GLM-5.2 API key configured with BigModel endpoint, (4) start.sh symlinked from orama-system to workspace. Deployment verified operational.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_252c22b599e0 -->
- On macOS/BSD, singleton daemon-start locks in shell scripts must use mkdir-is-atomic + rm -rf for teardown, never flock(1) or rmdir.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_8df4d109ae3a -->
- gbrain embedding configuration CRITICAL: use Ollama bge-m3 (local, no API key) NOT OpenAI. Provider=openai, model=bge-m3, base_url=http://localhost:11434/v1, api_key=dummy. Matches code-review-graph unified embeddings (same vector space). Session 2026-07-04: fixed 2114+ sync failures by switching from OpenAI (missing OPENAI_API_KEY) to local Ollama.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_a9c5357fb5ca -->
- gbrain sync failures can accumulate (2114+ in one session). When sync blocks: check ~/.gbrain/sync-failures.jsonl to see actual error, run 'gbrain doctor' for health status, fix root cause (usually config or API key), clear old state (rm ~/.gbrain/sync-*.json), then retry sync. If error is 'git pull failed', likely due to protocol.file.allow git security setting — not a blocker, sync continues on full reimport.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_b7daeff3b5b6 -->
- gbrain and code-review-graph BOTH use bge-m3 embeddings (1024-dim, local Ollama). Unified vector space = semantic search consistency across both tools. gbrain config: provider=openai (misnomer, means OpenAI-compatible API), model=bge-m3, base_url=http://localhost:11434/v1. CRG wired via MCP .mcp.json to same Ollama endpoint. ADR-045 recommendation: keep both systems on bge-m3 for semantic coherence.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_0876cd56a7cf -->
- gbrain sources can become stale (last synced 3+ days ago). Warning: 'Source X last synced Nd ago — brain search is stale!'. Fix: run 'gbrain sync --source <id>' to re-sync specific source, or 'gbrain sync' to sync all. Session 2026-07-04: Perpetua-Tools had 2300+ skipped files but 17 pages imported successfully after config fix. Stale index doesn't mean broken—just means search results may be out of sync with latest code.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_515332eceaa9 -->
- gbrain multi-source drift occurs when pages exist at 'default' source but should be at intended source (e.g., intended=gstack-code-XXX). Doctor reports: '2487 page slugs appear at default but NOT intended'. Root cause: pre-v0.30.3 putPage misroutes OR source never completed initial sync. Fix: re-sync with 'gbrain sync --source <id> --full' or delete misrouted page with 'gbrain delete <slug>'. gbrain team planning 'gbrain sources rehome' cleanup command (v0.32.0+).  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_113e9d0e1606 -->
- Fable-5 LLM Council model fallback priority: Tier 1 = Ollama qwen3.5:9b-nvfp4 (local, always first), Tier 2 = SKIP Windows GPU (offline), Tier 3 = GLM-5.2 BigModel (fallback), Tier 4 = Sonnet 5 Medium (absolute last resort only). Timeout: 10s per tier. No further fallback after Sonnet fails.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_6741dab9176f -->
- [PROVISIONAL] Fable-5 Git-Rebase Safety: Never use rev-list --count, merge-base, or ahead-behind to judge divergence post-rewrite; always use git cherry with tree-twins from git_reanchor_scan.sh to classify branches as ORPHAN/TWIN/NEEDS-REANCHOR. Consensus: 7/7 subagent types (Architect, Reviewer, General, CodeGuide, Explorer, ModelSelector, Validator). This prevents zero-tree-orphan-merges (LINT-200 100% pass), zero force-pushes to protected branches, and zero branch-collision incidents across concurrent multi-agent sessions.  <!-- status=provisional confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_17a8cb51d2b4 -->
- [PROVISIONAL] Fable-5 Tier-Based Routing: Each tier in frugality_router.py must enforce hard 10s timeout via killable background call (run_in_background=true, never bundled timeout N && cmd). Enforce fail-closed cost gate (Tier2+: gate() raises on deny, no silent reroute). Validate worst-case bounded latency <=40s (4 tiers) vs interactive SLA. Consensus: 7/7 subagent types. This prevents silent cost-gate bypasses (LINT-400 100% pass), cascade failures from tier timeouts, and SLA violations.  <!-- status=provisional confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_1bff8045670e -->
- [PROVISIONAL] Fable-5 MCP Diagnostics: Implement single-command health probe across all registered MCP servers (CRG, gbrain, alphaclaw-mcp, ai-cli-mcp) with <10s response time. Catches tool degradation within 1 invocation before cascade. Detects stale-name failures (detect_changes vs detect_changes_tool) and down-server escalation. Consensus: 6/7 subagent types. Emit OTEL span in router trace stream. Foundation: orchestrator/mcp_diagnostics.py mirrors connectivity.py endpoint_online() cache (one source of truth).  <!-- status=provisional confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_1a984f855042 -->
- [PROVISIONAL] Fable-5 Endpoint Centralization: Collapse dual discovery paths (lan_discovery.json vs last_discovery.json) into single canonical ~/.openclaw/state/last_discovery.json. Never hardcode Win LAN IP or manual probe; lan_discovery.py becomes producer only. All consumers (connectivity.py, backend_resolver.py, frugality_router.py, startup_intelligence.py) read from canonical file. Discovery freshness <=30s (launchd com.orama.network-watch watcher). Consensus: 6/7 subagent types. This eliminates 0 connection-refused/wrong-host incidents from DHCP churn and prevents parallel-probe anti-pattern (LINT-500 zero hardcoded IPs).  <!-- status=provisional confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_4f75a476b961 -->
- [PROVISIONAL] Fable-5 Tool Cataloging: Auto-discover 100% of skills/MCP/agents (70+/100+) with zero manual registration. Generate docs/generated/tool-catalog.json unioning SKILL.md frontmatter + MCP roots + worker_registry live regs. Runtime availability reuses connectivity.py endpoint_online() cache (single source of truth). Registry-stale tools flagged by CI before commit (LINT-302). Catalog-first fail-closed tool selection. Consensus: 5/7 subagent types. Foundation: ECC 2.0 fragmentation-detection precedent (docs/v2/ D21).  <!-- status=provisional confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_04f59c541076 -->
- [PROVISIONAL] Fable-5 Doc Layering: ADR pointer sync 100% (sync-docs-v2-pointers.sh --check green, LINT-600). Zero hand-edits to generated Layer-1 pointers. Cite D-number not ADR-NNN in skills (LINT-601). Skill wrapper canonical-path pointers resolve + fresh within N days of referencing CLAUDE.md sections. Never hand-edit LESSONS.md generated by learn.py; use learn.py pipeline for all new entries. Consensus: 5/7 subagent types. Establishes two-repo grounding mechanically via repo_hygiene.py drift detection.  <!-- status=provisional confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_b7064fb5b34e -->
- [PROVISIONAL] Fable-5 Real-Data-First Validation: Require paired docs/LESSONS.md live-run entry (endpoint, tier, timing via learn.py) as precondition before any Fable-5 v1 pattern is cited as justification in v2 (docs/v2/) planning doc. Cache-correctness diff-harness must pass green on live Fable 5 calls before semantic eval (LINT-700). Cost_guard validated against live budget ceiling (LINT-701). Affinity fail-closed CANCELLED+{policy:true} verified on Win RTX3080 (LINT-702). Consensus: 7/7 subagent types. Gates v2 promotion with measurable, auditable evidence, not assertion.  <!-- status=provisional confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_0133a39f4fc2 -->
- [PROVISIONAL] Fable-5 Skill Creation & Priority Roadmap: Six new consensus skills target Phase 0-1 stabilization: fable5-git-rebase-safety, fable5-tier-based-routing, fable5-mcp-diagnostics, fable5-endpoint-centralization, fable5-tool-cataloging, fable5-doc-layering. Phase 0 unblocks v2 multi-agent workflows (ranks 1-3, medium-small effort, existing foundations). Phase 1 hardens routing/catalog/docs (ranks 4-7, medium-large, triggers Phase 3 real-data-first gate). Phase 2 (concurrency coordination, telemetry aggregation) depends on Phase 0-1 solid. Top upgrade: model-routing-check skill gap (reachability vs actual router resolution), mcp-orchestration diagnostics subsection, hardware-policy timeout unification. Consensus: Architect + Reviewer + General + CodeGuide (highest-tier skill steering group).  <!-- status=provisional confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_58ff85d8cd5f -->
- Fable-5 LLM Council session complete (7 subagents, 7 patterns analyzed, 572k tokens): Top 5 skill upgrades (model-routing-check HIGH, mcp-orchestration HIGH, hardware-policy MEDIUM, endpoint-centralization MEDIUM, real-data-first MEDIUM), 6 orama adoption patterns (tiered dataclass, fail-closed gate, dual-track wrappers, live-derived status, zero-fragmentation, MultiLLMRouter decorator), 7 new skills proposed (git-rebase-safety, tier-based-routing, mcp-diagnostics, endpoint-centralization, tool-cataloging, doc-layering, real-data-first-gate). Consensus heat map: 7/7 on routing+safety, 6/7 on diagnostics+centralization+layering+gate, 5/7 on cataloging. All ready for v2 implementation.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_996849f37566 -->
- Live execution validation is non-negotiable for quality gates — static path checks and imports do not catch execution errors (e.g., cost formula mismatches, tier examples that raise exceptions). Re-review must test against real backends.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_20cb4caf591e -->
- Tier-based routing with flat cost formula 0.001 × est_tokens works across all escalation tiers (no per-tier variation). Each tier must enforce hard 10s timeout via killable background call. Cost gate raises on deny, no silent reroute.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_597850c7f05c -->
- Parallel subagent fixers work well when scopes are independent (e.g., code example fix vs. contradictions removal). No collision. Both approved on re-review without re-do cycles. Validation must serialize (re-review after all fixers).  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_906a46baec0a -->
- ECC submodule upgrade cycle: preserve 3 local-only files via patch + sync script (ecc-submodule-sync.sh restore). Files: frontend-design openai.yaml, .antigravity/ANTIGRAVITY.md, .gemini/ANTIGRAVITY.md. Patch tracked in scripts/git/ecc-local-additions.patch. Always run restore after submodule update.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_525743857a6b -->
- PR descriptions, docs, and memory files are append-only historical records: preserve the original corpus at the top, add updates below in a dated append-only log. Never replace the original purpose with the latest delta. Creating companion files in side directories (instead of appending to canonical LESSONS.md) repeats the clobber anti-pattern being documented — the remedy must not repeat the disease. Cross-ref orama-system docs/LESSONS.md §2026-07-07.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_910ea4c1f284 -->
- ~~When tracked files contain paths that look absolute (OS-specific home-directory path forms, OS-specific home-directory path forms), use relative paths instead (../../, ../) resolved from the file's own location within the repo. Never use username-containing placeholders or env vars that still expose directory structure (workspace-tree env-var path). The filename alone is sufficient when marked local-only; strip the parent path tree entirely. Standard: relative paths from the file's directory, or ~ for home, or $OPENCLAW_ROOT for the repo root. Applies to all tracked files including .agent/memory JSON and markdown.~~  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_4e93f2434ae0 superseded_by=lesson_645bf5262c23 -->

Use relative paths (../../, ../filename), env vars ($OPENCLAW_ROOT, $REPO_ROOT), or ~ tilde. LINT-006 in repo_hygiene.py and CIDF Target Verification both enforce this as a pre-commit hard gate. The same rule applies to AI-generated memory content: use relative paths or strip the parent path tree; do NOT use <user> placeholder (it still exposes the directory structure).~~  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_4dd8a22181c2 superseded_by=lesson_97dae564849a -->
- Canonical repo clones must not exist under duplicate paths like <duplicate>/<OS-specific-home-directory-path>  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_75d2cefe4ec8 -->
- When a cross-platform infra fix (line endings, encoding, build config) is documented in the wiki, implement it in main the same session — do not leave it as documentation only.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_9032452bf8c9 -->
- A batch script that switches branches, cherry-picks, or force-pushes against a shared canonical repo checkout must run in an isolated git worktree (git worktree add --detach <path> origin/main), never cd into the live directory other sessions/agents are actively using.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_5b40f288ce47 -->
- After orama-system's history is rewritten, verify a specific commit's content survived by diffing its tree against origin's current tree (git show <sha>:<path> | diff), not by trusting commit-graph ancestry (merge-base/ahead-behind) or the reanchor_scan classification alone.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_b19ffe0be141 -->
- When a new GPU node (Windows or Mac) joins the LAN fleet, write its onboarding steps as one self-contained downloadable markdown that composes the existing canonical installers (first-run-install.sh, ensure_requirements.ps1) rather than re-deriving install steps inline — keeps a single source of truth and lets the next node's real gaps get fed back as doc edits instead of tribal knowledge.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_0d4176661e36 -->
- graduate.py/learn.py/recall.py/show.py print unicode arrows (→) that crash on Windows' default cp1252 console encoding; force UTF-8 stdout/stderr at each script's entry point (sys.stdout.reconfigure) rather than removing the unicode.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_a96de386f4f5 -->
- Concurrent agents working on the same git repo cause .git/index.lock collisions on every commit, checkout, and stash. The orama-system using-git-worktrees skill exists specifically for this: both agents writing to the same repo → use a worktree. During the 2026-07-08 session, 15+ git operations failed with index.lock errors because another agent was committing to the same orama-system checkout simultaneously. Worktrees would have eliminated every single lock collision.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_19c8a7822ccd -->
- git filter-repo removes the origin remote as expected behavior after rewriting history. After scrubbing contaminated history with git filter-repo --replace-text, you must git remote add origin <url> again before pushing. The --replace-text option with a rules file (key==>REDACTED) is the correct way to scrub a secret from all blobs across all commits. filter-repo also expires reflogs and prunes unreachable objects automatically.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_da96b542e63e -->
- .gitattributes CRLF enforcement (eol=crlf on .cmd/.bat files) blocks git rebase and git cherry-pick when old branch commits modified those files with different EOL. The workaround is to temporarily mv .gitattributes .gitattributes.bak AFTER git checkout (since checkout restores .gitattributes from the tree), then restore it before pushing. Even this is fragile: git checkout -B restores .gitattributes, so the mv must happen after checkout, not before. For batch reanchor scripts, use set -uo pipefail without -e so individual branch failures do not abort the entire run.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_1fea952eb159 -->
- Hardcoded API keys in tracked files are a SECURITY.md violation. The BigModel GLM-5.2 API key was hardcoded in setup-glm52.sh and SKILL.md across 4 tracked files and 8 git commits. Per SECURITY.md: Read keys from environment variables, not source or tracked config. Always use $ENV_VAR or interactive read prompt, never echo literal-key > file. When a key is found in git history, the correct response per SECURITY.md leak-response is: (1) generate+deploy replacement credential, (2) verify it works, (3) revoke the exposed credential, (4) audit usage, (5) remove from active code, (6) git rm --cached if tracked, (7) treat history cleanup as secondary. git filter-repo --replace-text scrubs the key from all blobs but does NOT un-leak it — rotation is mandatory.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_ab9056510ce8 -->
- ClinePass (Cline Credits via cline-pass/glm-5.2 through api.cline.bot) is the better default for coding tasks over OpenRouter free tier. ClinePass has no rate limits (vs OpenRouter free 50 req/day, 20 RPM), 1M context window, full reasoning + tool loops, and dedicated billing (Cline Credits at app.cline.bot/credits). Use openrouter/free only for lightweight routing/triage. The cline_exec MCP tool (cline_mcp_server.mjs) exposes cline-pass/glm-5.2 to Claude Desktop and Claude Code. Cline CLI auto-refreshes its WorkOS OAuth token (~12 min expiry) so no manual token management is needed. The cline CLI is invoked via: cline task --json --auto-approve true -c dir --thinking medium -P cline-pass -m cline-pass/glm-5.2 --timeout 600 --retries 3  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_6e96f366006c -->
- ~~Pre-push hooks reject rewritten history after git filter-repo. The orama-system pre-push hook runs repo_hygiene.py which checks author identities against an allowlist. After filter-repo rewrites all commits, some author SHAs change, triggering bad_author=15 rejection. Use git push --no-verify to bypass when the history was already validated by pre-commit hooks and the rewrite was a deliberate security scrub. The --force-with-lease=<branch>:<old-sha> pattern is mandatory for safe force-pushes: it records the expected remote SHA and refuses if someone else pushed in the meantime.~~  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_80a090176bc8 superseded_by=lesson_cc2a07b7f83f -->
- Cline CLI has no OpenAI completions endpoint — the hub daemon (ws://127.0.0.1:25463/hub) speaks a proprietary WebSocket protocol (GET /v1/models returns 404), and cline --acp speaks ACP not OpenAI completions. The upstream API (api.cline.bot/api/v1/chat/completions) is OpenAI-compatible but fronted by a short-lived WorkOS OAuth token (~12 min expiry) that only the cline CLI auth loop can refresh. Therefore cline-agent in OpenClaw is an agent-level binding not a model-provider binding: the agent uses openrouter/free for lightweight routing and delegates coding to cline CLI via exec/ACP/MCP bridges. This is fundamentally different from codex-agent which binds codex/gpt-5.5 as a native model because codex serve exposes an OpenAI-compatible app-server.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c58c891a3979 -->
- Never use 'git checkout -- <file>' to undo a small test edit on a file that also has real uncommitted work — it discards ALL uncommitted changes to that file, not just the last edit.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_e89515ced299 -->
- Before removing a config-writing function, grep every consumer of the values it writes across the whole codebase (not just the obvious module) to confirm nothing reads them back — dead-write code can look load-bearing.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_e8a561b6e4b3 -->
- On Windows, a script that must call another PowerShell script as a real separate process (not in-process) needs 'powershell.exe -File <script>', not the venv's python.exe — passing a .ps1 to python.exe is a silent no-op, not an error.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_32441ba109fd -->
- On Windows fresh installs, always probe both the hyphenated and space-separated winget install-directory naming for a given app (e.g. 'LM-Studio' vs 'LM Studio') — winget package IDs and the actual install folder name are not guaranteed to match.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_7d0320f7e371 -->
- On Windows Python 3.13+, prefer 'netifaces2' over the original 'netifaces' package — the original has no prebuilt wheel past Python 3.11 and fails to build from source without MSVC build tools; netifaces2 is a maintained drop-in fork (same 'import netifaces' API) with prebuilt wheels for current Python.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_4cba77672bef -->
- PowerShell's 'New-ScheduledTaskTrigger -RepetitionDuration ([TimeSpan]::MaxValue)' is rejected by Task Scheduler's XML schema even though it looks like the correct 'run forever' sentinel — use a large-but-bounded duration (e.g. New-TimeSpan -Days 3650) instead.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_ad5cd941ad68 -->
- PowerShell 'Set-StrictMode -Version Latest' turns '(Get-Item -Path Env:VAR -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue).Value' into a hard error when VAR is unset (Get-Item returns $null, .Value on $null throws PropertyNotFoundStrict) — always null-guard the Get-Item result before dereferencing .Value under strict mode.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_77e55cfd1d47 -->
- A cached path/config file (like .paths.ps1) written on a broken first run can permanently shadow a later fix unless the code explicitly re-validates the cached value against current reality and force-rewrites the cache when they diverge — just fixing the underlying bug isn't enough if something else still trusts the stale cache.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_f445c8c5670b -->
- start.ps1 must be run with --lan-peer (not just --no-open) for LAN cross-machine coordination to actually work -- without it, PT/orama/Portal stay bound to localhost only, and the control-plane token never gets synced from PT's persisted state.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_4cfad151e35c -->
- The control-plane auth scheme has two independent lanes plus an outbound-only override: PT lane (PT_CONTROL_PLANE_TOKEN env + Perpetua-Tools/.state/control_plane_token file), orama lane (ORAMA_CONTROL_PLANE_TOKEN / ORAMA_CONTROL_PLANE_TOKEN_LOCAL env), and ORAMA_CONTROL_PLANE_TOKEN_PEER (tried first on outbound probes only). auth_mode reports unset/pt_only/orama_only/joint depending on which lanes have candidates; joint means either lane's token unlocks both services.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_f0a26d721940 -->
- PowerShell's [Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable(name, value, 'User') writes to the registry but does NOT refresh the CURRENT process's own $env: view -- an already-running shell keeps stale values, and any child process it spawns inherits the parent's stale in-memory env block, not a fresh registry read. Only a genuinely new top-level process (e.g. a fresh Task Scheduler tick) sees the update.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_fc919d717ef2 -->
- coord_pulse.ps1 and coord_monitor.ps1 do NOT source .env.local (only start.ps1 does, via scripts/env/load-local.ps1) -- any cross-machine config set only in .env.local (tokens, MAC_IP, timeouts) will not reach these scripts or the Scheduled Task that runs them. Persist such values as actual Windows User-level environment variables, not just .env.local, if coord scripts need them too.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_f451fdd47f70 -->
- [PROVISIONAL] Cline instance map (2026-07-08 session): 4 Cline processes run on the Mac. (1) PID 51483 node cline from zsh terminal. (2) PID 51484 .cline main binary 66.8% CPU 619MB active session. (3) PID 44584 .cline --cline-hub-daemon auto ws://127.0.0.1:25463/hub. (4) PID 71165 cline_mcp_server.mjs spawned by Claude Code session 0a13d9d5 as MCP stdio bridge. cline-agent registered in openclaw.json allowAgents but NOT dispatched via gateway. No instances started/stopped in last 3h.  <!-- status=provisional confidence=0.95 evidence=1 id=lesson_d05c151e5302 -->
- ~~TO THE AGENT WHO WROTE .agent/lessons.md (2026-07-04 07:15, the repo's approved identity <<redacted-private-identity>>, AutoResearcher session): The correct move to record lessons is exactly one command: python3 .agent/tools/learn.py '<your claim>' --rationale '<why this holds>'. That stages, graduates, and re-renders memory/semantic/LESSONS.md automatically. Do NOT create any markdown file by hand in .agent/ — not at root, not anywhere. .agent/lessons.md does not exist in the AGENTS.md structure; nothing reads it; you created a phantom file. Note: the repo's approved identity <<redacted-private-identity>> IS a canonical approved identity (see § 6 Git Hygiene) — the 2026-07-04 friction was orama-system's repo_hygiene.py APPROVED_IDENTITIES set being out of sync with check_identity.sh (which already listed it), not an unapproved identity. Fixed 2026-07-10 in both repos' repo_hygiene.py.~~  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_3d7af7b587ad superseded_by=lesson_8c65ceb12c59 -->
- Pre-push hooks reject rewritten history after git filter-repo. The orama-system pre-push hook runs repo_hygiene.py which checks author identities against an allowlist. After filter-repo rewrites all commits, some author SHAs change, triggering bad_author=15 rejection. Do NOT reach for `git push --no-verify` as a routine fix — it disables every client-side hook (secret scan, attribution guard, banned-token check), not just the identity check. Correct remedy: re-run filter-repo with a `--mailmap`/`--name-callback` that remaps the changed SHAs to allowlisted identities so the hook passes honestly; only bypass with explicit, per-occurrence user approval, and re-run the full hygiene suite immediately after. The --force-with-lease=<branch>:<old-sha> pattern remains mandatory for the eventual safe force-push: it records the expected remote SHA and refuses if someone else pushed in the meantime.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_e0da0795a005 -->
- When dual_path_dispatch.py fails locally on Mac with 'Context overflow' due to openclaw agent workspace limits, bypass it by compressing the payload and dropping it directly into the peer inbox using lan_peer_assign.py drop.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_95140c00130c -->
- The Git repository root for orchestration is orama-system, not the parent OpenClaw directory, and start.sh does not support a --sync flag.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_12e4b4379cbd -->
- For intra-machine multi-agent coordination (concurrent Claude Code/kimi/cline sessions in different git worktrees on the same machine), reuse orchestrator/GossipBus (SQLite FTS5 event log) as a claim/registration board instead of building new infrastructure — it is already intra-machine by design (zero LAN dependency). The only real problem is db-path resolution across worktrees, solved via 'git rev-parse --git-common-dir' (every worktree of the same repo resolves to the same shared .git dir, zero new env vars needed).  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_fc6e8293ab26 -->
- kimi CLI's -p/--prompt headless mode is incompatible with --yolo (hard error: 'Cannot combine --prompt with --yolo'). -p mode is already non-interactive by definition (no approval loop exists to bypass), so drop --yolo entirely when scripting kimi -p calls.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_3e67d96a87f5 -->
- openclaw agent --session-key agent:<id>:<key> requires <id> to already be a REAL, registered agent (openclaw agents list) — an arbitrary/fictional id (e.g. 'review') fails with 'Agent "<id>" no longer exists in configuration'. For a one-off model call without a persistent agent, borrow an EXISTING agent's slot (e.g. agent:main:<ephemeral-key>) with --model as the override; for anything recurring create a real persistent agent via 'openclaw agents add' instead of hand-jq-editing openclaw.json (the live schema uses agentDir/workspace/tools.profile, not the model:{primary} shape an intuitive jq template would produce — mirrors the existing openclaw-cron-add invariant).  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_77958788207a -->
- When a concurrent agent session has uncommitted tracked-file changes in the SAME shared git checkout (not a worktree), 'git pull --rebase' and 'git rebase origin/main' both fail ('cannot rebase: you have unstaged changes') even if those changes are in files you never touch. Do NOT stash/discard another session's live uncommitted work. Instead: 'git merge origin/main' tolerates a dirty tree as long as the incoming changes don't conflict with the dirty files (often resolves to 'Already up to date' if your commit already sits on top); a plain 'git push' also works without needing a clean tree at all, since only pull/rebase require it.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_aaf424830928 -->
- User-stated policy: when a provider (e.g. google-antigravity/Gemini) supports both interactive web/OAuth login and an API key, ALWAYS prefer completing the web/OAuth login flow. An API key is backup/fallback only — never set one up preferentially or skip the interactive login step just because a key is available or easier to script.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_0cd82de45dbb -->
- Before dispatching a new agent onto a task in a repo with concurrent multi-agent activity, grep the codebase for actual callers of the module being built or extended, not just its own file and tests. Well-tested, spec-compliant modules can pile up with zero real integration if the orchestration layer they are meant to feed only exists as pseudocode in a spec doc.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_cb0365fa1e21 -->
- 'openclaw models auth login' takes --agent as a flag on the PARENT 'models auth' command, not on the 'login' subcommand itself — 'openclaw models auth login --agent X --provider Y' fails with 'does not recognize option --agent', the correct order is 'openclaw models auth --agent X login --provider Y'. This login command also refuses to run without a real TTY and cannot be scripted around ('requires an interactive TTY... use paste-token when token auth is available'); paste-token needs the raw token value, there is no mechanism to copy another agent's existing credential into a different agent's isolated auth store. The '--agent' flag on the separate 'openclaw agent' (singular, invocation) command works differently and IS valid there — verified both behaviors live, do not assume flag position is consistent across sibling openclaw subcommand trees.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_b796e59ac6cf -->
- Before applying a fix that uses a mechanism with a known-bad prior lesson (e.g. 'flock'), check whether the new usage is actually the SAME mechanism as the prior failure or just shares a name. lesson_8df4d109ae3a warns against flock(1), the util-linux shell CLI tool (absent on macOS by default); fcntl.flock() is Python's wrapper around the flock(2) syscall, present on both macOS/BSD and Linux, and is a different, safe mechanism. Conflating same-named-but-different mechanisms would cause either a false rejection of a safe fix, or worse, missing a real repeat of a past failure when the names differ but the mechanism is the same.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c6a0da9ac61c -->
- When two parallel agent lineages independently fix the same review findings, do not trust either lineage's own summary of what it changed or why the other is subsumed — read the actual current diff/code of BOTH before blending or discarding. A well-formatted 'blend verdict' doc with citations and confidence scores can still be wrong: it can go stale relative to a fast-moving branch (analysis written against an old HEAD, unaware of newer commits on the same lineage), and it can contain plausible-sounding but technically incorrect claims (e.g. recommending threading.RLock() inside async def code, or claiming a declared-but-unused variable is a functional fix).  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_5e19c4a443ec -->
- When CI's 'install dependencies' step hand-lists packages instead of running 'pip install -e .[dev]' (or equivalent), pyproject.toml's declared test extras and CI's actual installed set are two independently-maintained lists that silently diverge. A test file can import a real, locally-installed package (e.g. hypothesis, requests) and pass on every dev machine while failing collection entirely in CI. Fix both: add to pyproject.toml (correct declarative source for local dev) AND to CI's explicit install list (what actually runs), don't assume fixing one fixes the other.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_a9541e6bb74d -->
- A test that only asserts the outer/observable outcome of a code path (e.g. 'accepted is False') can pass vacuously even when the specific mechanism it's meant to verify never actually fired (e.g. a reputation-penalty call, a lock acquisition, a cache write). When adding a test for 'X happened as a side effect of Y', assert on X's own state directly (the ledger's score moved, the cache has an entry, the lock's internal counter changed) — not just that Y's return value looks plausible, which can be true for reasons unrelated to X.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_a20c04d17831 -->
- agentic-stack-agent-blend.sh conflict playbook (v0.9.0->v0.18.0 cycle): (1) dead-duplicate-line conflicts (PT has a stale naive computation immediately overwritten by a corrected one, upstream has just the clean line) -> take upstream. (2) naive-timestamp-interpretation conflicts -> keep PT's local-time-and-convert version, not upstream's assume-already-UTC shortcut, because PT's own historical writers used bare datetime.now().isoformat() which captures local time. (3) two independent same-line fixes (e.g. PT's path-sanitizer wrapper + upstream's tz-awareness fix) -> combine both, never pick one side, since either fix alone reintroduces an already-solved bug. (4) pure mechanical refactors (context-manager wrap, line reorder) -> take upstream. (5) new content merged into an existing non-blocked file that itself references a blocklisted skill (e.g. skills/_index.md gaining a '## brain' section) -> union everything except the blocklisted reference; the tool's path-level blocklist does not scan merged file CONTENT for blocked references, only new file paths -- a known gap.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_df55c3d3eeb1 -->
- The agent-coordination-heartbeat skill is globally available: canonical source is orama-system/bin/orama-system/skills/agent-coordination-heartbeat/SKILL.md, and the PT-side wrapper is .agents/skills/agent-coordination-heartbeat/SKILL.md (mirrors the existing git-history-surgery/hermes-harness thin-wrapper pattern).  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_f479b2e1d815 -->
- PT .agents/skills/*/SKILL.md thin-wrappers point to a canonical path (bin/orama-system/skills/<name>/SKILL.md) that does NOT exist inside Perpetua-Tools itself — PT has no bin/orama-system/ directory. The canonical skill actually lives in the sibling checkout ../orama-system/bin/orama-system/skills/<name>/SKILL.md in this workspace layout. When a wrapper's claimed path 404s, don't conclude the skill is missing — resolve it via the sibling orama-system repo before reporting a gap.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_1fe8e0825abf -->
- Before closing a stale/superseded PR as 'nothing to salvage', check its .agent/memory/candidates/graduated/*.json files individually (git show <branch>:<path> for each), not just its code diff. A superseded code change can still carry a graduated memory lesson recording a claim (e.g. 'skill X wrapper exists at path Y') that is factually false on current main — revealing a real gap worth fixing even when the rest of the PR is dead weight.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_180e96cfff87 -->
- When a vendored submodule (agentic-stack) ships upgrades to a directory PT customized in-tree (.agent/), the AlphaClaw reverse-merge precedent (upstream always prevails on the integration line, but local customizations sit on the RECEIVING end of a union merge, never the source) maps to file-level 3-way merges via 'git merge-file base ours theirs', not a branch-level reverse-merge, since .agent/ isn't its own submodule/branch. Tool: scripts/git/agentic-stack-agent-blend.sh (status/apply/promote); base SHA tracked in .agent/.agentic-stack-blend-state.json, seeded from .agent/install.json's original scaffold version.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_9d1013fedd41 -->
- Claude Code Agent-tool subagent isolation can leak: a live user chat message sent mid-turn (while a background Agent-tool subagent was running) appeared to reach that subagent directly, bypassing the main session entirely. Evidence: the subagent's response ('Fair callout. In the last turn there wasn't actually a case of me running two things sequentially that should've been parallel... I made one Read call, then one Bash call... whenever I have multiple independent lookups queued up, I should batch them') is a coherent, specific answer to the user's exact question ('why sequential dual voices, just fan them out in parallel next time?') -- a question that never appeared anywhere in the main session's own context and was never included in that subagent's prompt. The subagent should have had zero visibility into the live conversation; something routed the user's message to it anyway, one level deep.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_39b634f974c7 -->
- Agent-tool subagents run with isolation:'worktree' can see a STALE checkout of the branch relative to the orchestrating session's real-time state, and can hallucinate confident, specific-looking false claims from that staleness rather than reporting uncertainty. Observed: a subagent claimed 'this worktree's HEAD (65916c81) is a stale snapshot; main already carries commit 60636ad5 (P9 reorder buffer...)' -- but 60636ad5 was the orchestrating session's OWN just-pushed commit on a feature branch, never on main at all (git merge-base --is-ancestor 60636ad5 origin/main confirmed NO). The subagent's worktree checkout was simply behind the live branch tip; it inverted that into a false 'you're behind main' narrative stated with full confidence and specific SHAs, not hedged.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_aec0ea55f497 -->
- When resuming a previously-spawned Claude Agent-tool subagent, use SendMessage(to=<agentId>), never Agent() again — Agent() always spawns a fresh, unrelated agent with zero prior context.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_80225a223f41 -->
- A worktree-isolated subagent (Agent isolation: 'worktree') can be stale relative to the live branch if the orchestrating session commits/pushes after the worktree checkout was created — it can then hallucinate a confident, specific-looking-but-false git claim (e.g. 'main already has commit X') instead of expressing uncertainty. Always independently verify any subagent claim about branch/commit state via 'git merge-base --is-ancestor' before trusting it.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_4b95729ffb3d -->
- When a security/consensus module reports 'implemented and tested' with N passing unit tests, grep for its real call sites across src/ and orchestrator/ (excluding tests/) before trusting the docstring's production-status claim — a fully-tested module can still have zero production callers, meaning all the hardening work delivers zero live risk reduction until wiring lands.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_4b4e74419bc8 -->
- Before hardening a BFT/permissionless-style threat model (Sybil detection, k-buckets, reputation-decay, equivocation slashing) in this codebase, re-check the premise against the actual deployment topology (single operator's own LAN, 1-100 self-owned nodes across L1/L2/L3, no external adversary with economic incentive) — the plan file that proposes such a model may explicitly foreclose this question ('is this the right problem is already answered') rather than answer it.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_beb8754f2448 -->
- When multiple independent review voices (Codex + Claude subagents) disagree or one contains a verified-false claim, save every voice verbatim in its own file with inline [FALSE, see caveat] or [CORRECTION, ...] annotations rather than silently correcting, omitting, or only synthesizing a summary — preserves full auditability and lets a human see exactly which claims were checked and how.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_a36cfa62ffb9 -->
- To invoke Kimi (Moonshot AI) for a review/coding task, use the native ~/.kimi-code/bin/kimi CLI directly (kimi --output-format text -p '<prompt>'), NOT openclaw agent --model openrouter/moonshotai/kimi-k2.6 — the latter fails with 'Model override is not allowed for agent main' because openclaw's main agent has a model allowlist. Also: -p/--prompt cannot be combined with -y/--yolo (errors 'Cannot combine --prompt with --yolo'); omit -y for read-only review prompts.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_5e44eabf16dc -->
- Every backgrounded external CLI/agent dispatch (codex exec, kimi -p, an Agent/SendMessage subagent) needs an explicit 15-minute hard ceiling (Monitor with a kill -0 poll + force-kill on timeout), not just reliance on the run_in_background completion notification — a stalled process and a legitimately slow one look identical from outside until you check sustained CPU activity.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_cec9b1830125 -->
- Before wiring P5/P6/P13 security patterns into production, require a concrete witness/topology premise check against the current deployment; if there are no independent witnesses and no adversarial incidents, descope Byzantine/Sybil machinery to simpler liveness controls until the trust boundary changes.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_f1ab78c9a04c -->
- A bare 'cd <dir> && <command>' in this Bash tool persists the cwd change to EVERY subsequent command in the session — it does not scope to that one line the way a subshell would. When checking a worktree/branch other than your main working tree (e.g. 'cd .claude/worktrees/X && git status'), always cd back to the main repo root immediately after, or use 'git -C <dir> <cmd>' instead of a bare cd to avoid drift.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_a31e13420061 -->
- Concurrent 'git commit'/'git add' from multiple agent sessions on the same checkout produces two distinct failures needing different handling: (1) git's own index lock file contention (a 'File exists' error, or a security hook blocking that lock path) -- never delete that lock file yourself, retry the commit in a bounded loop instead, it clears on its own once the other process finishes; (2) 'nothing to commit' right after a git add -- the other session's commit already landed and cleared your staged changes between add and commit, so re-run git add immediately before retrying commit, not just the bare commit.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_89c71cb1930f -->
- A security/scout-block hook in this environment does a literal text-match on certain sensitive path substrings appearing ANYWHERE in a Bash command string, including inside commit-message heredocs and grep/read arguments -- it blocks the whole command even when the string is just being discussed in documentation prose, not actually operated on. If a git commit (or any command) gets blocked citing a path like a lock file that isn't really the problem, check whether your own commit message or command text literally contains that substring before assuming it's a real state conflict -- rephrase the wording (e.g. avoid writing the exact dotted path, describe the concept in prose instead) and retry.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_87be43ee3c1d -->
- When cataloguing an existing pattern/reference library against new work, only draw cross-references that are substantively real -- explicitly note which candidate files have NO genuine overlap rather than force-fitting every entry into a mapping table. A catalogue that honestly says 'no forced connection here' is more trustworthy and more useful to future readers than one that manufactures a link for completeness.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_5b6bd6b7d194 -->
- An append-only event log (GossipBus-style: emit() is a plain INSERT, state is derived by folding events) cannot provide compare-and-swap on its own -- a read-validate-then-emit sequence (like a claim-board's claim operation) has a real TOCTOU race between separate OS processes, no matter how tight the code looks. The fix is a small side table with a UNIQUE/PRIMARY KEY constraint on the contested key (e.g. task_id), used ONLY as the exclusion point (INSERT, catch IntegrityError) -- keep the existing business-logic validation (status checks, dependency checks) as-is, the side table just closes the gap after them. Remember to release the row on every path that makes the resource reclaimable again (retry/requeue), not just on final completion, or a legitimate future claim gets permanently blocked by its own stale exclusion row.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_5c30e65bf8d2 -->
- EnterWorktree's default base branches from the repo's fresh default remote branch (e.g. origin/main), NOT whatever branch you actually need to work on. If the task is to fix something on an existing PR/feature branch, the worktree starts on the wrong branch -- immediately fetch and checkout the real target branch inside the new worktree (git fetch origin <branch>:<branch> && git checkout <branch>) before making any edits, rather than assuming the worktree already has the right content.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_b88743907e4e -->
- Before switching git branches directly in a shared checkout, always run git status first -- if it shows uncommitted changes and this repo has other concurrent agent sessions active, those changes may belong to a live in-progress session, not stray state to discard. Do NOT git stash/checkout/reset past them. Per this repo's own CLAUDE.md § 8 ('create a worktree when the task requires parallel file writes by multiple agents'), spin up a worktree for your own task instead and leave the shared checkout untouched -- then fetch the actual target branch into that worktree if the task is against a specific existing PR/branch, not main.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_9f9dec0d3314 -->
- Perpetua-Tools' own shared-branch Git workflow was dogfooded by concurrent AI coding sessions: multiple sessions worked on the same reviewed branch, where one session's committed fix became another session's starting point through Git state. Do not infer unproven runtime queue, claim/release, heartbeat, or append-only event-folding usage from that evidence unless direct runtime evidence is attached.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_f256c7674a8e -->
- An unconflicted git auto-merge is not proof a local fix survived it -- git can silently resolve a hunk in favor of the 'wrong' side (reverting a prior local patch back to its pre-fix state) without ever flagging a conflict, if the two sides' diffs don't textually overlap in a way git's 3-way merge detects. After any merge that touches files with a known local-patch history (e.g. an .agentic-stack-blend-state.json applied_clean list), diff the post-merge content against the expected patched content directly -- don't trust 'no conflict markers' as proof the fix is still there.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_504ae339cf0b -->
- git merge-tree --write-tree takes exactly 2 positional branch args and auto-detects the merge-base; passing a 3rd positional arg silently produces a wrong/misleading result  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_be32d3a38cb6 -->
- GitHub's per-PR mergeable/mergeStateStatus fields only compare each open PR against current main, never against sibling open PRs, so two open PRs can silently conflict with each other even when GitHub reports both as individually mergeable  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_3dc6aae095d2 -->
- A git worktree's core.hooksPath can drift to an absolute path instead of the repo-relative .githooks, breaking any hook-verification script that does a strict string-equality check against '.githooks'  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_ce342af35284 -->
- A TODO's non-essential acceptance criterion (e.g. a benchmark with no real workload to test against yet) should be explicitly deferred with a cross-repo doc link, not silently dropped or left as an unchecked box with no explanation  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c1c47a25b008 -->
- On a shared branch with multiple concurrent agent sessions, always 'git fetch + git log HEAD..origin/<branch>' before starting substantial fix/review work — a gap you're about to fill may already be closed by a concurrent session, and duplicate work risks silent collision or wasted effort.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_a33c62ef731f -->
- To make a GossipBus.emit() atomic with another SQLite mutation (e.g. an exclusive-claim row insert/delete), use bus.transaction() to get a shared connection, bus.emit_within(db, event_type, payload) to insert the event without committing, commit once yourself, then bus.schedule_embed(row_id, safe_payload) afterward — never embed before the caller's own commit succeeds, since pre-commit data might still roll back.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_a89e267de1b0 -->
- A blind find/replace private-IP-sanitization pass can silently break embedded example code inside documentation (fixture values replaced with themselves become no-ops, two roles collapsed onto one identical placeholder): after any automated sanitization pass over docs with code blocks, do a manual correctness re-read of those blocks — don't assume 'sanitized' means 'still illustrative and correct'.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c382044a0f0b -->
- When two branches independently evolve 'the same' fix (e.g. two GossipBus atomic-transaction APIs), don't blindly port one branch's implementation onto the other — check the target branch's own established style first (e.g. PR206's BEGIN IMMEDIATE + bounded lock-retry vs PR205's simpler transaction()-only pattern) and match that, or you introduce an inconsistent API surface within one branch.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_79413f608a27 -->
- When a function's return tuple grows (e.g. insert_event 2-tuple to 3-tuple to 4-tuple), grep the entire codebase for all call sites that unpack the return value before changing the signature. A single missed caller that unpacks the old signature breaks CI silently. Cost of one missed caller exceeds cost of auditing all of them.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_519cff57e7ee -->
- asyncio.get_event_loop.run_until_complete raises RuntimeError no current event loop in Python 3.11 plus worker threads created by run_in_executor. A broad except Exception around it silently masks the failure leaving the singleton None. Fix: use asyncio.run(coro) instead which creates and tears down a fresh event loop deterministically. Cannot call asyncio.run twice in the same thread so design worker-thread init functions as one-shot.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_7cef6fb18db7 -->
- SQLite schema migration ALTER TABLE blocks must catch sqlite3.OperationalError duplicate column specifically, not bare Exception. Bare except Exception masks real migration failures such as disk full, corrupt DB, or locked table. Preserve idempotency with specific exception types; let everything else propagate to the caller.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_ad623fd69712 -->
- INSERT OR IGNORE with cursor.rowcount equals 0 is the correct idempotent dedup pattern for cross-peer event replay. When IGNORE fires on a duplicate event_uuid with a UNIQUE index, cursor.rowcount is 0 and cursor.lastrowid is stale — do not return it. Instead SELECT the existing row by event_uuid and return its id for idempotent operation. This avoids a race-prone SELECT-then-INSERT sequence.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_a0a25f26a06d -->
- The GossipBus insert_event canonical return is a 4-tuple of row_id, event_uuid, safe_payload, inserted. The inserted boolean is True for fresh rows, False for duplicates when INSERT OR IGNORE hit. Callers must check inserted before calling schedule_embedding so duplicate forwarded events skip embedding to avoid redundant work.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_e58908b7d323 -->
- Never delete integration test coverage without adding equivalent replacement tests elsewhere. CodeRabbit flags RC5 when tests vanish. When removing code, check test coverage first; if the removed code had tests, either keep the tests if the code still exists elsewhere, or rewrite them against the new location.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c505edaed079 -->
- Before any force-push or branch reset on an open PR, create a safety ref (tag or branch) pointing at the commit about to be discarded. Use git push --force-with-lease with the exact expected remote SHA recorded before the push. This ensures no unique work is lost even during history surgery on shared branches.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_f9eebf061831 -->
- Generated memory output such as LESSONS.md and lessons.jsonl must only be modified through the canonical memory pipeline (learn.py to stage, graduate.py to promote, render_lessons.py to regenerate LESSONS.md). Hand-editing these files is explicitly forbidden by the integrative merge and post-review remediation doctrine because it bypasses clustering, dedup, and metadata tracking.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_0615ce2d90ad -->
- When a force-push replaces the HEAD of an open PR branch with a commit that is already merged into the PR target branch, GitHub automatically closes the PR as merged. This is a reliable way to detect that the force-push landed the intended ancestor work into the base.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_e01e7ec74e74 -->
- When harmonizing two divergent branches, use the integrative merge doctrine: synthesize never amputate. Apply resolution modes in order: additive, union, superset, synthesize, architecturally-correct, api-correct. Archive instead of delete when content must leave the active path. Never pick one side wholesale with --ours or --theirs without classifying the conflict mode first.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_18e3dcbb5c1a -->
- Perpetua-Tools' own multi-agent coordination primitives (scripts/agent_coordination.py's GossipBus-backed distributed task queue: claim/release/heartbeat, append-only events folded into current state) got real-world load-tested by accident: while multiple independent AI coding sessions worked concurrently on PT's own codebase (this exact coordination system among the files being changed), those sessions ended up coordinating with each other THROUGH the very primitives they were improving -- one session's committed fix became the next session's starting point, purely via git state on a shared branch, no explicit hand-off. This is orama-system eating its own dogfood while working on its own self-improvement: the system under construction was, unintentionally, also the coordination substrate for constructing it.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_7d171138cc9f -->
- Perpetua-Tools' own shared-branch Git workflow was dogfooded by concurrent AI coding sessions: multiple sessions worked on the same reviewed branch, where one session's committed fix became another session's starting point through Git state. Do not infer unproven GossipBus-backed queue, claim/release, heartbeat, or append-only event-folding usage from that evidence unless direct runtime evidence is attached.  <!-- status=legacy confidence=0.7 evidence=0 id=lesson_legacy_077c77c2c2ed -->
- When using OramaSys skills from an OpenClaw workspace, resolve canonical skill cards from the checked-in orama-system repo under orama-system/bin/orama-system/skills, not from copied wrapper text or hardcoded workstation paths; run the wrapper's fetch/status/pull safety check before relying on the card.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_12a9bd0b9ab3 -->
- For PR remediation after a rogue or incomplete agent run, work newest blockers first, then audit backward through prior PRs; broadcast canonical methodology to subagents, keep vendor and ECC drift explicitly out of scope when requested, and only declare success after GitHub checks and local branch status are re-verified.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_83255f3cdcab -->
- Do not rely on broad async audit agents as the only source of truth when they may time out; if teammates stall, split audits into smaller bounded tasks and independently verify critical checklists with local code inspection and GitHub status before closing the loop.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_a7c88d1aa43c -->
- For StateTransitionManager reorder buffers, a buffered observation that expires before its sequence gap fills must be rejected as stale without advancing the applied watermark; advancing the watermark lets later successors skip a sequence that was never accepted and breaks stale-gap CI coverage.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_9cefe8d68f6f -->
- When applying CodeRabbit comments, preserve the underlying invariant rather than just the diff shape: canonical guard scripts must be changed at their source and synced, duplicate gossip events must skip expensive embedding work, duplicate payload reads must be sanitized, and migration handlers should catch sqlite3.OperationalError narrowly so unexpected failures propagate.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_eedb1f40f441 -->
- Before saying a PR or branch is ready, run two sync passes if requested: fetch, fast-forward pull when clean, push, inspect PR checks, then repeat a final no-op sync; report any intentionally ignored drift separately instead of hiding it.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_d0eac3060dc8 -->
- When CodeRabbit comments on .agent memory archival, sanitize each archived JSON entry before computing its duplicate/archive identity and persist that exact sanitized object; otherwise duplicate detection can disagree with the tracked JSON representation and path-hygiene guarantees.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_95760283c14b -->
- A stable facade over a legacy module must not accidentally re-export legacy implementations for paths that have been centralized elsewhere; import the canonical implementation directly and add identity/import tests for the facade exports that matter.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_38a8d498540e -->
- For subprocess tests that assert GitHub Actions guard output, specify encoding='utf-8' together with text=True so captured stdout/stderr decoding is deterministic across runners and locales.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c13f24c7f7cc -->
- When adding a regression test for ID-vs-persisted-representation bugs, make the fixture exercise the exact ID source field; if the ID helper prefers id/timestamp/event_id before hashing, a test that only changes a non-ID payload field can pass without proving the invariant.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_1701adf18da9 -->
- When salvaging a stale branch that has both already-merged patches and still-valuable follow-up work, run git cherry -v against the current base first and split the work by review story instead of rebasing the whole branch by reflex.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_bd7f5a38df5a -->
- For stranded branch cleanup, prefer two focused preservation PRs over one mixed cargo PR when the remaining commits naturally separate into product-plan work and general docs/housecleaning.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_4fd7156e6ae0 -->
- Before filing uncommitted changes into salvage buckets, classify dirty state by repo and risk; submodule drift that points at commits not present locally should be deferred rather than bundled into unrelated PRs.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_1516395f7701 -->
- If a local pre-push hook stalls after printing attribution rows, verify the branch with local checks and remote refs, then use --no-verify only for the push while documenting the reason; do not bypass hooks silently.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_036189f9a2e0 -->
- When splitting accumulated remediation work after a branch closes, classify every uncommitted or leftover change into an explicit bucket branch before opening PRs: feature-specific work gets its own branch, general housecleaning gets a separate branch, and each PR body must preserve a future-agent warning not to replace or delete the summary.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_31d190dba94f -->
- For Antigravity or Gemini-style headless agents, run a sandboxed plan/read-only pass first and require explicit human confirmation before switching to accept-edits or launching background edit fan-out; never treat sandboxing or headless mode as sufficient approval.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_78b0efde1631 -->
- Do not write agent fan-out logs to predictable absolute /tmp paths; create a secure unique log directory under the repo or approved user tool state, reject symlinked reusable parents, and keep stdout/stderr per task for audit.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_27354b2896b1 -->
- When a user says a remediation branch is closed and asks to commit to main, switch the target repo to main, fast-forward from origin first, ignore explicitly excluded vendor or ECC drift, then commit only intentional memory or doc changes.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c7a96af333af -->
- When a cross-repo implementation plan spans orama-system code and Perpetua-Tools memory, link artifacts by repo name, PR number, branch name, and repo-relative path only; sanitize any local source path to env anchors like $HOME and use the canonical memory pipeline instead of hand-editing generated lesson files.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_b22b588ac3b2 -->
- Before opening preservation PRs for every unmerged branch, first list remote branches not merged into origin/main and existing open PR heads; create PRs only for remote branches with no open PR, and include a future-agent warning to append rather than replace the PR summary.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_f82b8720eed5 -->
- When preserving many unmerged branches, process remote branches separately from local-only branches; local-only branches require an explicit push/sync step and may be unsafe or hook-blocked, while remote branches can get PRs immediately.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_b260bd87eb89 -->
- G7 async notifications in orama-system must remain a v1 portal MVP: preserve versioned SSE envelope fields for future v2.1 GossipMesh adapters, but do not implement mesh replication, durable replay, or v2.5 safety overlays in the G7 MVP branch.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_8560a9654f72 -->
- Use python3, not python, in this macOS workspace when running PT/orama tests unless the repository explicitly provides a python shim.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c10dae30315a -->
- After context compaction, browser refresh, or a long-conversation reset, resume from committed working-memory records and live Git state before relying on an older surviving chat exchange.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_72c5dec9dd21 -->
- When a user provides a verified Firecrawl installation, successful smoke test, research question, authority filter, output shape, and time cap, execute the bounded research pass immediately; do not repeatedly ask for setup or depth that is already supplied.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_24350aa70ab2 -->
- When a constrained agent can reconstruct and write indexes but cannot run PT memory tooling, a capable PT session must verify the Git evidence, preserve the report as user-reported provenance, run the supported memory tools, and explicitly name any operation the original session did not execute.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_51061c3300bf -->
- When reorganizing cross-repo plans, preserve history with git mv and maintain a directed authority graph: canonical docs feed active plans, active plans feed research and implementation handoffs, and archive material routes back to active authority.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_bcae4fa52d1c -->
- When auditing Markdown links, skip fenced code and custom URI schemes, preserve append-only historical ledgers, and repair only resolvable live-document paths; otherwise a code literal or historical record can be mistaken for a red link.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_278bd77811ef -->
- Treat launcher diagnostics as capability tiers: --validate must exit before environment loading, bootstrap, discovery, probes, writes, or service startup; --list may read existing configuration but must parse path-cache files without sourcing them, redact tokens and non-loopback hosts by default, and require explicit opt-in before revealing network topology. Keep shell and PowerShell contracts behaviorally symmetric while preserving platform-native lifecycle operations.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_24032d26aecc -->
- When a plan cites an internal authority doc (e.g. SECURITY.md §N) as justification for a design decision, verify the citation exists as written before trusting it — search the target file, all branches, and git history for the exact phrase, not just the section number  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_5eebf9b451f5 -->
- Test fixtures that hand-build synthetic data instead of calling the real transformation function under test can hide production bugs that pass every test — verify test doubles call the actual function (e.g. the real redaction/serialization step), not a fixture shaped to match what the test author assumed the output would look like  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_822ea3166668 -->
- When dual-voice adversarial review (independent Claude subagent + Codex) converges strongly against a plan, steelman the critiques before accepting the verdict — some adversarial findings dissolve on inspection (holding an MVP to a bar it explicitly disclaims in its own stated constraints), while others survive because they're verified against actual source code, not just argued  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_8ba8fd2be37f -->
- A decision logged as 'accepted into scope' in a review's audit trail is not the same as that fix actually landing in the reviewed artifact's code — verify the fix is present in the actual code blocks/diff, not just recorded as a table row, before treating a plan or PR as ready  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_4a0d575a955a -->
- Before applying a proposed security fix that reuses an existing auth/origin-check primitive, read that primitive's actual implementation and any documented design rationale — a function whose name suggests it blocks cross-origin requests may deliberately allow the specific origin you're trying to block, by design, for legitimate cross-service reasons  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c67ec2e4ceea -->
- The 'decision accepted but never coded' failure mode recurs across review phases even after being caught once — each subsequent phase must independently re-verify that ALL prior phases' accepted fixes actually landed in the artifact, not just the fixes that phase itself is focused on  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_3e05027815a2 -->
- When fixing a test to route through a real function instead of a synthetic fixture, verify every downstream assertion's indexing/shape assumptions too, not just the input construction — changing what data flows in without checking what the assertions expect out leaves a differently-broken test  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_63c3a8bb65e0 -->
- Async generators are lazy — code inside them (including subscription/registration side effects like hub.subscribe()) does not run until the generator is first iterated, not at generator-creation time. Emitting/triggering an event before a consumer has actually started iterating a lazily-created generator silently drops that event rather than erroring  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_f32db7f8cd4e -->
- When testing an infinite-streaming (SSE) FastAPI/Starlette route, check for @app.middleware('http') decorators before assuming the test's own code is buggy -- that decorator registers Starlette's BaseHTTPMiddleware under the hood, which re-streams every response through an internal call_next() anyio memory-stream bridge that has a well-documented hang with infinite streaming bodies. No client-side httpx/TestClient timeout can interrupt it since the hang is server-side inside the middleware  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_0f6905cc7d56 -->
- For coordinated PR repair, always read the live job board and recent GossipBus events before acting; stale plan files are not enough when another agent may have dropped conflict or ownership hints.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_9b5f3cd393bd -->
- When a review-fix PR has drifted behind current main, reconcile main first and resolve conflicts by synthesis, then apply reviewer comments; do not stack fixes on a stale branch.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_ad58015aee6c -->
- A shell fix that must guarantee a specific environment value (e.g. forcing UTF-8 locale before parsing tracked text) needs an unconditional assignment, not a ${VAR:-default} fallback — the fallback only fires when the var is unset/empty, so a caller's pre-existing conflicting value silently survives and the fix's stated guarantee is never met.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_478769875d35 -->
- When a git merge conflict inside a tracked patch/diff file is purely a hex-abbreviation-length mismatch in an 'index a1b2..c3d4' line (7-char vs 8-char, no real content difference), do not hand-pick a side — regenerate the artifact via its own generator command and re-approve. core.abbrev-driven length can legitimately differ run to run; only the freshly regenerated form is guaranteed self-consistent with what the tool will produce next time.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_120a7509b06d -->
- Do not fire a manual git push immediately after a backgrounded shell command that itself ends in git push — check whether the background task actually completed first (via its notification/output), or the manual push races the async one and 'remote rejected: cannot lock ref, is at X but expected Y' looks like a real branch-divergence conflict when it is only local ref-tracking staleness from your own in-flight push.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_1d583879f00d -->
- For Exa MCP on OpenClaw machines, configure Codex as a stdio MCP pointing at orama-system/scripts/exa/exa-mcp-wrapper.sh, not as the bare https://mcp.exa.ai/mcp URL. The wrapper resolves EXA_API_KEY from OpenClaw config or macOS Keychain, shares one exa-mcp-daemon with Claude/orama-system/Perpetua-Tools/Codex, and exposes web_search_exa plus web_fetch_exa after a new Codex session loads MCP.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c44a4a1a614d -->
- A freshly-written process doctrine can be violated by its own author within the same session it was written — always back a doctrine with mechanical enforcement (a pre-push hook, a CI check), not just documentation, or the doctrine's own authoring is not exempt from the failure mode it was written to prevent.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_1a8b5403e949 -->
- Before any destructive git ancestry operation (force-reset, history rewrite, branch deletion), create a safety ref pointing at the commit about to be discarded first, unconditionally — this is what turns a scary 'we might have lost work' moment into a routine cherry-pick-and-reset, and costs nothing to do even when it turns out to be unnecessary.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_bb8f39c02526 -->
- When two files cover overlapping content, do not assume the newer or smaller one is a duplicate of the older or larger one — check derivation direction (which came first, which was derived from which) before proposing any merge or consolidation, since merging an origin doctrine into its own later derivative is backwards even though the content genuinely overlaps.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c95aba599c4c -->
- Git author identity is not a reliable signal for distinguishing human from autonomous-agent authorship in a stack where agents commit under whatever git identity is locally configured — cross-check autonomous-agent activity logs alongside git log before attributing a commit to a human based on the author field alone.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_3ad9e2ace20c -->
- A tool with multiple entry paths into the same durable record (e.g. an auto-derived path and a manual-override path) needs identical side effects on every path, or referential-integrity gaps will surface much later via something unrelated to the original write, rather than at the point where the gap was actually introduced.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_c0b337199888 -->
- Do not fix a bug about untrustworthy manual bypasses of a tool by making another manual bypass of that same tool — implement the tool fix first, then dogfood the fixed tool itself to record any further lessons, so the fix's own credibility is demonstrated rather than undermined.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_d09ee4ff5162 -->
- When preparing a patch against a real external repository for eventual PR submission, always work from an actual git clone or fetch of that repository, never from files reconstructed via a raw-content or API fetch into a fresh git init -- matching file bytes is necessary but not sufficient, since GitHub's compare/PR view requires a real shared commit ancestor, and local checks like syntax validation or unit tests will pass even when the git ancestry itself is fabricated.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_b3e46d32d10d -->
- When wrapping a launchd daemon that sources a user's real shell profile (.zshrc/.bashrc) for env setup, do not put 'set -u' (nounset) around that sourcing — a zsh-authored profile hits variable patterns bash's nounset treats as an unconditional fatal abort the instant it's sourced, which is not a normal non-zero exit and bypasses both 2>/dev/null and any || fallback chain entirely. The script dies silently before reaching any of its own logic, with no error surfaced anywhere.  <!-- status=accepted confidence=0.6 evidence=1 id=lesson_f07f22229c53 -->
